Kanpur, the 23rd December 1970 
S . O . 1658. - - A licence No. P / S / 1633308 dated the 26th June, 1969 for the 
" value of Rs. 2. 00, 000 for the import of Permissible Spare parts as shown in ap 
pendix 32 о the Import Trade Control Red Book Vol. I for April, 1969 — March , 
1970 licensing period was issued to M / s . Progressive Industries, 103 / 285 , Colonel 
ganj, Kanpur . 

Thereafter a Show Cause Notice No. Enf. I ( 309 ) / 1969 Kan dated the 15th 
NOctober . 1970 was issued asking them to show cause within 15 ( fifteen ) days of 
the receipt of notice as to why the said licence in their favour should not be 
cancelled on the ground that they had no facilities for service and maintenance 
in their factory and the licence was, therefore, not to serve the purpose for 
- which it was issued . 

No reply to the said show cause has so far been received and the stipulated 
" period for reply has expired . 

The undersigned has carefully considered the case and has come to the con 
clusion that the licence in question will not serve the purpose for which it was 
" Issued . 

Having regard to what has been stated in the preceding para the undersigned 
is satisfied that the licence in question should be cancelled or otherwise rendered 
ineffective. Therefore , the undersigned , in exercise of powers vested in him 
under clause 9 sub - cleuse ( cc ) of the Imports ( Control ) Order , 1955 hereby can 
cells the licence No . P / S / 1633308 dated the 26th June , 1969 for Rs. 2 . 00 . 000 
Issued in favour of M / s . Progressive Industries, Kanpur.

. [ No. ENF .I( 309 ) / 1989 / KAN. ]: 
एस प्रो० 1659.-- - सर्व श्री राजेन्द्र मैटल इन्डस्ट्रीज , 111/ 448 , हर्षनागल , कानपुर 
को अप्रैल , 1969 -मार्च, 1970 लाइसेंस अवधि के लिए प्रायात व्यापार नियंत्रण रै बुक के वालुम - 1 
के परिशिष्ट 32 में दिए गए के अनुसार स्वीकन फालतु पूजों के प्रागत के लिए 2,00, 000 रुपये 
का पायात लाइसेंस सं० पी / एप्स 1633201दिफ 31 - 5 - 6 9 स्त्रोक : किया गया था । 

तत्पश्चात् , एक कारण बतानो नोटिस सं० ई एन एफ 1 ( 309 ) / 1969/ कान दिनांक 15. 
अक्तूबर , 1970 यह पूछते हुए की गई थी कि नोटिस प्राप्त होने के 15दिनों के भीतर कारण बताएं . 
कि उनके नाम में जारी किया गमा उक्त लाइसेंस क्यों न रद्द कर दिया जाय और उसे इस आधार पर 
कि उनके कारखाने में सेवा तथा अनुरक्षण संबंधी सुगमता नहीं थी और इसलिए जिस उद्देश्य 
हेतु लाइसेंस जारी किया गया था , उसे पूरा नहीं करता था । 

उक्त कारण बताओ नोटिस के लिए अभी तक कोई उतर प्राप्त नहीं हना है पीर बताई गई . 
निर्धारित अवधि समाप्त हो गई है । 

अधोहस्ताक्षरी ने मामले की भली भांति जांच कर ली है और इस परिणाम पर पहुंचा है कि 
जिस उद्देश्य हेतु विषयाधीन लाइसेंस जारी किया गया था वह उसे पूरा नहीं करेगा । 

पूर्व की कंडिका में जो बताया गया है उसे ध्यान में रखते हुए अधोहस्ताक्षरी इससे संतुष्ट 
है कि विषयाधीन लाइसेंस रद्द अथवा अन्यथा रूप से अप्रभावित किया जाना चाहिए , इसलिए 
अधोहस्ताक्षरी आयात (नियंत्रण ) प्रादेश, 1955 की धारा 9 उपधारा ( सी० सी० ) के अन्तर्गत 
प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए लाइसेंस सं० पी० एस० / 16 3 3201 दिनांक 31 - 5 - 19691 
मूल्य 2, 00,000 स्पये जो सर्वश्री राजेन्द्र मंटल इन्डस्ट्रीज , कानपुर के नाम जारी किया गया था ,, 
एतदद्वारा रद्द करता है ।

$ . 0 , 1600 , - A licence No. P / S / 1651389 dated Ist September, 1969, for the value 
of Rs. 8850 for the import of German Silver Scrap was issued to M / s . Indian 
Sanitary Ware , Chauraha Gali , Moradabad . 

___ Thereafter a Show Cause Notice No. Enf . I ( 290 ) / 1969 / Kan dated the 3rd 
Noveniber , 1970 was issued asking them to show cause within 15 ( Fifteen ) days of 
the receipt of notice as to why the said licence in thei. favour should not be can 
celled . on the ground that they had no factory in existence and the licence was, 
therefore, not to serve the purpose for which it was issued . 

M / s. Indian Sanitary Ware, Moradabad replied to the said show cause and 
stated that they were not interested in the said licence due to its small value. 

The undersigned has carefully considered the case and has come to the con 
clusion that the licence in question will not serve the purpose for which it was 
issued . 

Having regard to what has been stated in the proceding para the undersigned la 
satisfied that the licence in question should be cancelled or otherwise rendered 
ineffective . Therefore, the undersigned , in exercise of powers vested in him under 
clause I Sub - clause ( cc ) of the Imports ( Control ) Order , 1955 hereby cancells 
the licence No. P / S / 1651389 dated the 1st September , 1969 , for Rs. 8 , 850 issued 
in favour of M / s . Indian Sanitary Ware , Chaurahagali, Moradabad .

का पुर 24 दिसम्बर, 1970 
एस० ओ० 1650 . - सर्वश्री इन्डियन सैनिटरि वेयर , चौराहा गली , मुरादाबाद को जर्मन 
सिल्वर स्क्रेप के प्रायात के लिए 8, 850 रुपये का प्रायात लाइसेंस सं० पो / एस 165 1389 
दिनांक 1 - 9 - 1969 स्वीकृत किया गया था । 

तत्परमान , एक कारग प्रो जोडा . ई एन एक -- - 1 ( 290 ) / 196 ) कान , 
दिनांक 3, नवम्बर, 1970 यह पूछने हर जारी की गई थी कि नोटिस प्राप्त होने के 15 दिनों 
के भीतर कारण बाएं उनके नाम में जारी किया गया उक्त लाइसेंस क्यों न रद्द कर दिया 
जाना चाहिए और उसे इस प्राधार पर कि उनका कोई भी कारखाना विद्यमान नहीं था और 
इसलिए जिस उद्देश्य हेतु लाइसेंस जारी किया गया था , उसे पूरी नहीं करता था । 

सर्वश्री इन्डियन सेनिटरि , मरादाबाद ने उक्त कारण बताओ नोटिस का उत्तर दिया 
और कहा है कि वे उक्त लाइसेंस में किसी प्रकार को दिलचस्पी नहीं लेते थे, क्योंकि लाइसेंस 
का मूल्य बहुत कम था । 

अधोहस्ताक्षरी ने मामले की भली भांति जांच कर ली है और इस परिणाम पर 
पहवा है कि जिस उद्देश्य हेतु विषयाधीन लाइसेंस जारी किया गया था वह उसे पूरा नहीं 
करेगा । 

पूर्व की कंडिका में जो बताया गया है , उसे ध्यान में रखते हुए अधोहस्ताक्षरी इससे संतुष्ट 
है कि विषयाधीन लाइसेंस रद्द अथवा अन्यया रूप से प्रभावित किया जाना चाहिए । इसलिए , 
अधोहस्ताक्षरी पायात (नियंत्रण ) आदेश , 1955 की धारा 9 उप -धारा ( सी सी ) के अन्तर्गत 
प्रदत्त अधिकारों का प्रयोग करते हुए लाइसेंप सं० पी एस / 1651389 दिनांक 1 - 9 - 1969 
मल्य 8, 850 रुपये जो सर्वश्री इन्डियन सेनिटरि वेर चौराहाली, मुरादाबाद के नाम में जारी 
किया गया था , उसे एतद्वारा रद्द करता है ।

SUBJECT. — Cancellation of Licence No . P / S / 1636028 / C / XX / 84 / X / 29 - 30 , dated! 

22 - 3 - 1970 for Rs . 5 , 000 both the coples. 


s . o . 1664. - - M / s . The Mysore Palace Agarbathi Works . Goripalyam,. 
Bangalore - 18 , were granted import licence No. P / S / 1636028 / C / XX / 34 / X / 29 - 30 . 
dated 22nd March , 1970 , for Rs. 5 , 000 for import of Aromatic Chemicals and N . L . 
Olls . They have now applied for duplicate copy of Customs purpose copy as well. 
as Exchange Control purpose copy of the above licence on the ground that the 
original of the above Customs purpose and Exchange Control purpose copy of the 
licence have been lost . It is further stated that the above licence is not at all . 
utilised and the duplicate Customs purpuse licence copy and Exchange Control 
purpose copy of the licence new required is for Rs. 5 ,000, 

2 . In support of the above contention , the applicant have Aled an affidavit. I 
am satisfied that both the original Customs purpose and the Exchange Control 
purpose copies of the above licence have been lost and direct that a duplicate copy 
of the Customs purpose copy and Exchange Control purpose copy of the above: 
licence should be issued to the applicant, for the full valuc of Rs. 5 ,000 only . The 
original Customs purpose and the Exchange Control purpose copy of the licence 
are hereby cancelled ,

MINISTRY OF TOURISIM AND CIVIL AVIATION 

New Delhi, the 12th April 1971 


S . O . 1667. – Whereas on the 26th March , 1971 , a Jamair Co. Private Ltd ., Dakota . 
aircraft VT-ATT while on a passenger flight from Gauhati to Calcutta via Bagdogra 
crashed about 30 miles East - North East of Hashimara, resulting in the desth of 11 . 
passengers and 4 crew ; 


And whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is expedient to hold 
formal investigation in the circumstances of the said accident; 


Now, therefore, in exercise ot the powers conferred by ru. c 75 of the Aircraft :. 
Rules , 1937, the Central Government hereby di : ects that a formal investigation of 
the said accident be held ; 


The Central Government is further pleased to appoint Shri S . K . Dutta , retired 
Judge of the Calcutta Iligh Court, to hold the said investigation , 


The Central Government is also plcased to appoint: 
(1 ) Group Cant. J . R. Bhasin, Director of Flight Safety , Bir Fleadquarters, 

___ New Delhi , and 

( 2 ) Capt. B. S . Sareen, Retired Regional Director , Indlan Airlincs, New Delhi ,. 
to act as assessors to the said investigation .

सा० प्रा० 166 7 - यतः जामयेर कम्पनी प्राईवेट लिमिटेड का डकोटा विमान वी टी - ए० टी०टी० 
एक यानी उड़ान पर बागडोगरा के मार्ग से गोहाटी से कलकत्ता जाते हुए हाशिमारा के लगभग 30 मील 
पूर्व- उत्तर पूर्व में दुघटनाग्रस्त हो गया , जिसके परिणाम स्वरूप 11 यात्रियों और विमान कर्मीदल के 
4 सदस्यों की मृत्यु हो गई । 

और यतः भारत सरकार की दृष्टि में इस दुर्घटना की परिस्थितियों के बारे में प्रौपचारिक जांच 
किया जाना उचित प्रतीत होता है ; 

अतः, अब, विमान नियम 1937 के नियम 75द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए , केन्द्रीय 
सरकार एतद द्वारा निदेश देती है कि उक्त दुटना की प्रौपचारिक जांच की जाय ; 

केन्द्रीय सरकार उक्त जांच करने के लिये कलकत्ता हाई कोर्ट के अवकाश प्राप्त न्यायाधीश , . 
श्री एस० के० दत्ता की नियक्ति भी करती है ।

( 1 ) ग्रुप कैप्टन जे० आर० भसीन , निदेशक , सुरक्षा उड़ान, वायु सेना मुख्यालय, नई 

दिल्ली ; और

MINISTRY OF FOOD , AGRICULTURE , COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT AND 

CO -OPERATION 
( Department of Agrloulture ) 

New Delhi, the 27th March 1971 
8 . O . 1681, -- Whereas a draft of the Cotton Grading and Marking Rules , 1970 
was published , as required by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce (Grading and 
Marking ) Act, 1937 ( 1 of 1937 ) , at pages 3151 to 3154 of the Gaze ie of India , 
Part II, Section 3 , Sub - section (it) , dated the 25th July , 1970 , with the notification 
of the Government of India in the Ministry of Food Agriculture , Community 
Development and Cooperation , (Department of Agriculture ) , No . S . O . 2459. dated 
the 10th July , 1970 , inviting objections and suggestions from all persons likely to 
be affected thereby , till the 10th August, 1970 . 

And whereas the said Gazette was made available to the public on the 28th 
July , 1970 ; 

And whereas no objections or suggestions have been received from the public 
on the said draft; 

Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 3 of the said 
Act and in supersession of the Cotton Grading Marking Rules , 1962, the Central 
Government hereby makes the following rules , namely :-- 

RULES 
1. Short title and application. — ( 1) These rules may be called the Cotton Grad 
ing and Marking Rules , 1971. 

( 2 ) They shall apply to the varieties of cotton which are grown in India and 
which are specifed in Schedule I . 

2 . Definition . - In these rules, 
(1) " Schedule" means a Schedule annexed to these rules, 
(2 ) " False or fraudulent packing means packing cf a bale of cotton ; 
( a ) in such a manner as to contain in different parts of the bale , cotton of 

different growths or varieties, or cotton of materially different staples , 

or cotton of different crops ; or 
(b ) in such a manner as to contain in any part of the bale any byeproducts 

such as waste , fly , or other byeproducts of a cotton mill available 
after raw cotton is passed through the blow room ; or

3 . Grade Designation and quällty . - ( 1 ) The grade designations of the varieties 
of cotton specilied in Schedule I shall be as specified in column 1 of Schedule II 
and the special characteristics and the general characteristics indicated by the 
grade designations shall be specified in column 2 and 3 respectively of Schedule II. 

( 2 ) The grade designations referred to in sub -rule ( 1 ) shall be applied only to 
cotton in full pressed bales. 

4 . Grade designation marks . — ( 1 ) The grade designation mark shall consist of 
a label specifying the grade designation and bearing a design ( consisting of an 
outline map of India with the word AGMARK and the figure of the rising sun with 
the words " Produce of India " ) resembling, that set out in Schedule III.

5 . Method of Marking: - ( 1 ) The grade designalion mark shall be placed against

( 3 ) The affixing of the grade designation mark on an end hessian or an un 
lashed side of a bale shall not be deemed to fulfl the requirements of this rule .

customary in the trade .

. खास, कृषि, सामुदायिक विकास तया सफा रता मंत्रालय 

(कृषि विभाग ) 
नई दिल्ली , 27 मार्च 1971 


का० प्रा० 1681 – यतः कृषि उत्पाद ( श्रेणीकरण और चिह्न ) अधिनियम , 1937 
( 1937 का 1 ) की धारा 3 की अपेक्षानुसार कपास श्रेणीकरण और चिह्न नियम 1970 
का प्रारुप भारत के राजपत्र भाग 2, खंड 3, उपखण्ड ( II ) तारीख 25 जुलाई , 1970 में पृष्ठ 
3155 -- 3160 पर , भारत सरकार के खाद्य कृषि , सामुदायिक विकास तथा सहकारिता मंत्रालय 
( कृषि विभाग ) की अधिसूचना संख्या का० प्रा० 2459 तारीख 10 जुलाई, 1970 के साथ 
उन सभी व्यक्तियों से 10 अगस्त , 1970 तक आक्षेप और सुझाव मामंत्रित करते हुए, जिनका 
एतद्वारा प्रभावित होना संभाव्य था , प्रकाशित किया गया था । 

और यतः उक्त राजपत्र जनता को 25 जुलाई, 1970 को उपलब्ध करा दिया मया था । 
और यतः उक्त प्रारुप पर जनता से कोई प्राक्षेप या सुझाष प्राप्त नहीं हुआ है । 

अतः उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 3 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, और कपास 
श्रेणीकरण औरचिन्न नियम , 1982 अधिकान्त करते केन्द्रीय सरकार एतदद्वारा निम्नलिखित 
नियम बनाती है, अर्थात् :- - 


1. संक्षिप्त नाम और लागू होमा : -- 

( 1 ) ये नियम कपास श्रेणीकरण और चिह्न नियम , 1971 कहे जा सकेंगे । 
( 2 ) ये कपास की उन किस्मों पर लागू होंगे जो भारत में उगाई जाती है और जो अनसूची 

- अनुसूची 1 में विनिर्दिष्ट हैं । 
2. परिभाषा : - - इन नियमों में , 

( 1 ) " अनुसूची " से इन नियमों से उपाबद्ध अनुसूची अभिप्रेत हैं ; 
( 2) "मिथ्या या कपटपूर्ण पैकिंग " से कपास की गांठ का निम्नलिखित रीति से पैकिंग अभि 

प्रेत है : - -- 
( क ) ऐसी रीति से पैकिंग की गांठ के विभिन्न भागों में विभिन्न उत्पत्तियों या किस्मों 

की कपास या तत्वतः विभिन्न रेशों की या विभिन्न फसलों की कपास अन्तर्विष्ट हो । 
प ) ऐसी रीति से पैकिंग की गांठ के किसी भाग में कोई उपोत्पाद जैसे रद्दी, फलाई ( उड़न ) 

या कच्ची कपास के विधूलन कोष्ट ( कलो रुम ) से निकाले जाने पर उपलब्ध काटन 

मिल के अन्य उपोत्पाद अविष्ट हो ; या 
( ग ) ऐसी रीति सेकिंग की गांठ के किसी भाग में कच्ची कपास से भिन्न कोई छिपे 

हुए, पदार्थ अन्तर्विष्ट हों ; या 
( घ ) ऐसी रीति से किंग कि इसमें की भीतरी की कपास विनिश्चित रुप से बाहर की 

कपास से घटिया हो और रुढिगता परीक्षा से उसका आसानी से पता न चल सके । 
3. श्रेणी अभिषान और क्वालिटी :- - (I ) अनपूची 1 में विनिर्दिष्ट कपास की किस्मों 
के श्रेणी अभिधान वे होंगे जो अनुपची II के स्तन । में विनिर्दिष्ट हैं और श्रेणी अभिधान द्वारा 
उपदशित विशेष और साधारण लक्षण वे होंगे जो अनुसूची II के क्रमशः स्तंभ 2 और 3 में विनि 
दिष्ट हैं ।

( 2 ) उपनियम (। ) में विनिर्दिष्ट श्रेणी अभिधान केवल पूर्णतः कसी हुई गांठ की कपास 
पर ही लागू होंगे ।

अभिधान विनिदिष्ट हो और अनुसूची IIT में दिए गए डिजाइन ( जिसमें एगमार्क शब्द के साथ भारत

एक डिजाइन होगा । 

( 2 ) डिजाइन और श्रेणी अभिधान चिन्ह निम्नलिखित रंगों के होंगे ।

5. चिन्ह की पद्धति :----( 1 ) श्रेणी अभिधान चिन्ह गांठ के चपटे पाश्वों में से श्रेणी में से 

किसी एक पर लगाया जाएगा, टाट के रेपर ( लपेटने ) पर चिपकाया जाएगा । 

और न्यूनतम 3 हफों ( पट्टीबन्धों ) से सुरक्षित स्थिति में रखा जाएगा । 
( 2 ) श्रेणी अभिधान चिन्ह गांठ करने की तारीख कपास की किस्म और उत्पत्ति का 

स्थान ( ब्लाक जिला राज्य ) स्पष्टत : दशित करेगा । 
( 3 ) टाट के किनारे पर या गांठ के बन्ध हीन पार्श्व पर लगाया श्रेणी अभिधान चिन्ह इस 

नियम की अपेक्षाएं पूरी करता हुश्रा नहीं समझा जाएगा । 
6 . पक करने की पद्धति : - - ( 1 ) कपास गांठों में व्यापार में रूढ़िगत रीति सेपैक की जाएगी । 
( 2) गांठों की कोई भी मिथ्या या कपटपूर्ण पैकिंग नहीं होगी ।

ऐगमार्क प्रमाणित 

( काला लेबल ) 


( क ) उस कपास ( विनौलायुक्त रूई ) से ( क ) कपास की मशीन 

व्युत्पन्न उत्पाद होगी जो अनुसूची 1 में मोटाई से प्राप्त लिन्ट 
उपशित तथा किसी सरकारी फार्म में या ( पूर्णतः कसी हुई गांठों 
समुचित सरकारी विभाग द्वारा अनुज्ञप्त में ) के रूप में होगी ; 
किसी रजिस्ट्रीकृत बाज उत्पादक द्वारा या ( ख ) साफ और संयुक्त 
इस प्रयोजन के लिए कृषि विषपन सलाहकार रूप से पत्ती , विनीले 
से मान्यता प्राप्त किसी अन्य अभिकरण धब्बे या अन्य अपूर्णतामों 
द्वारा उत्पादित हो और जो किसी सरकारी से मुक्त होगी ; 
बीज डिपो या इस प्रयोजन के लिए कृषि ( ग ) सूखी और किसी 
विपणन सलाहकार से मान्यता प्राप्त किसी शमान भी बढ़ाई गई

New Delhi, the 12th April, 1971 
S . O . 1686 . - -In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act. 1947 ( 14 
of 1947 ) , the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of the 
Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad , in the industrial dispute bet 
ween the employers in relation to the Dhanşar Colliery of Messrs Pure Dhansar 
Coal Company, Post Office Dhansar, District Dhanbad and their workmen , which 
was received by the Central Government on the 6th April , 1971.

DHANBAD

APPEARANCES : 

For ih ? Tirployers Shri S . S . Mukherjee, Acivocate , with Shri B . Joshi, 

Advocate. 
For the Workmen - Shri Lalit Burman , General Secretary, Bihar Koyla

Dhanbad , dated the 3rd April . 1971 

AWARD 
The above Reference arises out of the Order of the Central Government under 
clause ( d ) of sub -section ( 1 ) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, 
being order No. 1 ,25 68 -LRII, dated New Delhi, the 11th October , 1968 by which 
the following dispute was referred to this Tribunal : 

SCHEDULE 
" ( i ) Whether the management of Dhansar Colliery of Messrs Pure Dhansar 

Coal Company, Post Office Dhansar , District Dhanbad was justified in 
dismissing S |Shri Jago Gope and Kishun Singh , Underground tram 
incrs, with effect from the 14th August, 1968 ? If not, to what relief 

are these workmen entitled ? 
( ii ) Whether the management of Dhansar Colliery of Messrs Pure Dhansar 

Coal Company, Post Office Dhansar , District Dhanbad is justified in 
not making payment of profit sharing bonus to the workmen for the 
accounting year 1966 - 67 ? If not, to what relief are thc workmen 

entitled ? " 
1 . I intend to deal with the two items separately . At first , I take up the ques 
tion of dismissal, which is the subject matter of the first item . The concerned 
workmen at all relevant times were the employee under the management of 
Dhansar Colliery of Messrs Pure Dhansur Coal Company owned by partners, 
The workmen say that the concerned workmen were the permanent employees 
of the said collicry with a clcan record of service over a long period . According 
to them the management deprived the workers of the colliery of legitimate benefits 
like quarterly bonus, profit sharing bonus, provident fund , leave wages, sickness 
allowance etc . Their further case is that as the management refused to imple 
ment thc recommendations of the Central Wage Board , which were to be imple 
mented froni thc 1st January , 1967, there was a great discontent among the work 
ers of the collicry , who organised a branch of the Bihar Koyla Mazdoor Sabha in 
the month of July , 1967 to ventilate their grievances through their organisation , 

2 . Shri Jago Gope and Kishun Singh , the workmen concerned were elected 
they say , as President and Secretary respectively of the Dhansar Colliery branch 
of the union and their lawful trade union activities were much disliked by the 
employers who were constantly trying to break the unity of the workers and to 
disrupt the union . 

3 . In paragraph 5 of the Workmen s written statement it has been stated that 
on the face of organised movement of the workers and as a result of the inter 
vention of the Labour Department the management had to agree to the impli 
mentation of the Coal Wage Board recommendations only from the 15th August 
1967. Mr. Burman , appearing on behalf of the workmen , said at the hearing 
that " 1967" was typing mistake, that it should be " 1968." An application for 
Amendment, properly verified , should have been Alcd in time. As that has not 
been done I shall proceed on the footing that it is " 1967 " as given in the written 
statement and not " 1968" as suggested by Mr, Burman . 

4 . They accuse the management of victimising the local organisers of the 
urion for pulling pressure on the management to implement the l Écommonda 
tions of the Central Wage Board for Coal Mines , which they ultimately agreed 
to do with clect from the 15ih August, 1967 . The relevant portion from thcir 
written sateinent is quoted below : " while the management agreed to implement 
the Wage Board recommendations only from 15th August 1967 decided to 
victimise tha ior : ! organism of the Union from 14t1 August , 1907, i. e . jurt one 
lay before the date of implementation of Wage Board i r commendations with a 
clear malafide intentions to terrorise tn othcr vonlaus and in cofre the other 
lcading organisers of the Union . " Mr. Burman at the time of hearing isked me 
to read " 1563 " for " 1967 " on the grourd of typing mistake, but for the reason 
already stated I shall procecd on the footing that it is " 1967" as given in the 
written statement and not " 1968" as suggested by Mr. Burman . 


2078 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24 , 1971 /VAISAKHA 4 , 1893 [Part II 


5 . The workmen have challenged the enquiry proceeding in these words: 
" That the management did not hold any proper enquiry over the charge and 
did not grant any reasonable opportunity to defend the affected workmen and 
the manageinent has dismissed the affected workmen on the basis of perverse 
findings , which is not legally maintainable , because the management has violated 
the principles of natural justice in dismissing the affected workmen with a clear 
intention to victimise the affected workmen " . 

6 . The employers version is as follows. On 20th July 1968 and again on 22nd 
July 1968 Jago Gope and Kishun Singh , the workmen mentioned in item No. 1 
of the schedule of reference along with two others, who were all under ground 
trammers threatened to assault the attendance clerk while he was issuing oil to 
them . For the above misconduct individual charge sheet dated 26th July 1968 
were issued to the workmen concerned and two others who threatened to assault 
the attendance clerk . All the four workmen submitted a joint reply dated 28th 
July 1968 denying the charges. A departmental enquiry was held in the presence 
of the said workmen on 8th August 1968 when they were given full chance 
and opportunity to cross examine the witnesses for the management and produce 
dcfence witnesses , 

7 . It is the definite case of the employers that the said four workmen includ 
ing the two concerned in the present case participated in the enquiry by cross 
examining wilrcsses and producing one witness in dcfcnce . They , however , say 
that the workmen or the defence witness did not put their left thumb impression 
on the minute of the enquiry proceeding. Their further case is that in the above 
departmental enquiry the misconduct mentioned in the charge sheet, datod 26th 
July , 1968 WAS Saii factorily established against Jago Gope and Kishun Singh 
the workmen concerned, who were, therefore, dismissed by a letter dated 14th 
August, 1963 , 

8 . First of all let me consider whether the departmental enquiry was held . 
In their written statement the workmen have sald that the managcment did not 
hold any proper enquiry over the charges and did not grant any reasonable op 
portunity to workmen concerned to defend themselves. It is not their case in 
the written tatcment that enquiry was not held at all. But at the hearing Mr. 
Burman on their behalf took up the position that there was no enquiry , Materials 
on the record clearly establish that an enquiry was held . 

9 . A joint notice dated 26th July , 1968 was served on the two workmen con 
cerned and two others to show cause why , disciplinary action would not be taken 
against them ( Ext. M —- 1 ) . All the four workmen sent a joint reply , dated 28th 
July , 1988 , denying the allegations (Ext. M — 2 ) . All the four workmen were in 
formed by the Manager by a letter dated the 31st July , 1968 that an enquiry 
into the chargesheet would be held on the 6th August 1968 at 10 A . M . in the 
Manager s omice , That this letter was received by the workmen concerned is 
proveä by their documents Exts . W6 and W7, both dated 7th August 1968. W7 is 
a letter written by Jago Gope to the Manager which runs as follows: " With 
reference to your letter No. . . . . dated the 31st July , 1968 informing me that the 
enquiry into the charge - shcet . . . . will be held at 10 A . M , on the 6th instant, I 
have to inform you that although I presented myself for the enquiry no such 
enquiry was lield " . Ext. W6 is also to the same effect. Ext. W4 is a letter dated 
6th August, 1968, written to Kishun Singh , one of the workers concerned , by 
the Manager to say that as he did not appear for enquiry on the plea that he 
would tender apologies and as no apology was subsequently submitted enquiry 
vould be held at 10 - 30 AM , on the 8th August, 1968 in the Manager s office , 
Ext. W5 is a similar letter addressed to Jago Gope . These two letters must have 
been received by the workmen concerned because they have been filed by them . 
Thicre was no denial to the allegations made in Exts, W4 and W5. Though 
Fxts W6 and W7 were written by the concerned workmen on the 7th August , 
1968 , there was no mention of the letter of the manager dated 6th August , 1968 . 
Exts . W6 and w7 were evidently written with the idea of utilising them in 
future to show that no enquiry was held . 

10 . I have no doubt in my mind that an enquiry was held on the 8th August. 
1968 and that the concerned workmen took part in that enquiry, MW1 at first 
Syys that company issued notice for holding enquiry on two days and that on 
both the days he and others presented themselves for the enquiry . Then he 
says that no enquiry was held . Ile also says that he and others wrote 8 letter 
to the management informing that no enquiry had been held . He evidently 
refers to Exts WⓇ and W7. One would have expected similar letters from the 
workmen concerned if no enquiry had been held on the 8th August . 1968 . On the 
other hand , MW1, Malvern in his deposition has clearly stated that he held the 


- - 


.. . 


- . - 


. - . ... 


- 


Sec . 3 (ü ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24 , 1971 / VAISAKHA 4 , 1893 2079 

- - 
enquiry on the 8th August in the presence of the concerned workmen and two 
others and that they were given full opportunity to cross- examine all the 
management witnesses . The enquiry proceeding, Ext. M10 has also been filed . 
I have no reason to disbelieve Malvern nor have I any reason to think that 
Ext. M10 was fabricated subsequently . Having regard to the admitted docu 
nients in relation to the charge sheet and the enquiry and having regard to the 
relations between the parties it is highly improbable that the concerned work 
men were dismissed without any enquiry whatsoever and without giving the 
worker opportunity to cross - examine the witness produced by the management, 
On the evidence on record I am satisfied that an enquiry into the allegations 
made in the charge - sheet was held on 8th August 1968, that the concerned work 
men and two others scrved with the charge sheet participated in the said 
enquiry and that they had full opportunity to cross - examine the management 
witnesses and to produce their own witness . 

11 . The next point for consideration is whether the finding of the enquiry 
officer that the concerned workmen are guilty of the acts of misconduct men 
tioned in the charge sheet is supported by the materials on record . One witness 
Danrath Mahto , was produced by the workmen charged with misconduct at the 
departmental enquiry . He deposed as follows: " I heard some alteration between 
Jago Gope und Kishun Singh and Hazree Babu about chargc - sheet. . . . I did not 
hear anybody abusing when I was at the attendance room ." He had earlier 
said that trammers had gone down the mine carlier than the inincrs. The con 
cerned workmen are trammers and the witness is a miner . The enquiry officer 
has given the following rcason for disbelieving him : " He stated that he did not 
hear any -body abusing Sri Lachman Munda, but he confirms that some alter 
cation was going on between Jago Gope, Kishun Singh and Lachman Munda , 
statement of Dashrath Mahto is contradictory and hence cannot be relied ." In 
my opinion he was not justified in rejecting his cvidence on the ground stated 
by him . In the first instance , the statement of Dashrath Mahto cannot be said 
to be contradictory . If there is some altercation betwecn two personis it does 
not necessarily follow that one will abuse another . The statement of this wit 
ness amounts to this : there was altercation between Kishun Singh and Jago 
Gone on the one hand and Lachman M .1. 110 on the other , bu : the workmen conn 
Cl _ led did not abuse Lachnan Mundu , ihe attendance cierk , It should be remem 
bercd that Dashrath Mahto was not at all cross - examined on behalf of the man 
agement. The enquiry officer was not justified in rcjccting the cvidence of 
Dashrath Mahto . 

12 . The attendance clerk , Lachman Munda said as follows in his examination 
in chief regarding the incident of the 22nd July , 1968 ; " They again abused me 
on 22nd July 1968 in III shift while I was issuing oil to them because I rcported 
the incident of 20th July 1968 to the Manager , " He did not say that the work 
men concerned threatened to assault him . Yet the cnquiry officer says that 
" Shri Lachman Munda also complained of having been threatened by Shri Jago 
Gope and Kishun Singh again on 2nd July 1968 while issuing oil to them in 
III 3niſt." Jagdish Prasad improved upon the statement of the attendance clerk 
by saying that Kishun Singh and Jago Gope abused the attendance clerk and 
tireatonci to break his hands and legs. He was deposing regarding the inci 
dent or the 22nd July , 1968 . In his cross - examination he confcssed that he did 
not know on what matter Kishun Singh was abusing the attendance clerk . 

13. As to the incident of 20th July , 1968, the evidence of Baijnath Prasad 
practically proves nothing. In his examination in chief he said , " When I came 
to attendance room Jago Gope and Kishun Singh were abusing and threatening 
Lachman Munda . I asked these persons as to why they are abusing attendance 
clerk . " The work threatening is rather vague. He did not say that they were 
threatening to assault , which was in the charge sheet. Immediately thereafter 
he said that he asked the persons as to why they were abusing the attendance 
clerk . This shows that by the expression abusing and threatening , he really 
Trieant abusing . But in the charge sheet the misconduct mentioned in threat to 
assault. So , the enquiry officer is not justified in saying that Baijnath Prasad 
corroborated the testimony of the attendance clerk and of M . P . Paswan , It is 
clear that the enquiry officer was labouring under a misconception that the 
Words " threatened the attendance clerk " and the words " threatened to assault 
the clerk ” mean the same thing . It is needless to point out that the word threat 
is rather vague, while the words threat to assault mean something definite , 

14 . The nature of the finding is such that it at once strikes one as biaset 
For the reasons stated above and also on the ground of blas I am constrained 
to hold that the finding of the enquiry officer is perverse, and is liable to be set 
aside ,

15 . In my opinion the order of dismissal too has not been passed in accord 
adow WIulie standing order s Cläuse ( IV ) of Standing Order No. 17 reads as 
follows: " În awarding pinahm ist undcr this Standing order , the authority 
awarding punishment shail Tüke mi account the gravity of the misconduct , the 
previous rocoru , if any , ol lile Woikinan and any other exccnuating or aggravat 
ing circumslences that may exist . . . . ” Exts, W8 and W9 are the letters of dis 
missaí addressed to the concerned worknien . They are in identical language 
These letters of dismissal were issued over the signature of D , P . Lodha , Partner , 
for Pure Dhansar Coal Co . There is nothing in any of these two letters of dis 
missal which shows that the authority awarding punishment, namely the part 
ners of Pure Dransar Coal Co., took into account the gravity of the misconduct , 
the previous record , if any, of the workmen concerned and other extenuating 
or aggravating circumstances that might exist. The relevant portion of Ext. W8, 
letter of disinissal issued to Kishun Singh , iş quoted below : " With reference to 
charso sheet No . . . . . and reply thereto , a departmental enquiry was held on 
8th August, 1968 in your presence when you were given full chance and oppor 
tunity to cross - examine the witnesses and defend yourself. In the above depart 
Ireniai wnquiry the misconducts mentioned in the above charge sheet have been 
satisfactorily cstablished . You are , therefore , dismissed from service with im 
mediate ellect." 


16 , Shiva Kumar Lodha , MW2 admitted in his cross - examination that he did 
nou know whether the entire maitur was considered by the other partners before 
passing order of dismissal against Kishun Singh and Jago Gope. The docu 
mencs filed by the management e . g . Ext . M14 , show that the colliery is owned 
by four partners , namely , ( 1 ) Sm . Nanhi Bai Lodha , ( 2 ) Shiva Kumar Lodha, 
( 9 ) Debi Prasad Lodha , and ( 4 ) Baijnath Lodha . Both the letters of dismissal 
were signed by D . P . Lodha as partner for Pure Dhansar Coal Co, D , P . Lodha 
obviously stands for Delhi Prasad Lodha, Evidence on record shows that the 
question of dismissal was not considered by all the partners in the light of 
clause ( iv ) cf Standi:" g Order No . 17 . The order of dismissal is liable to be set 
asice in this grourd also, namely , that it was passed in violation of Standing 
Order No. 17 liv , 


17 . Suich consideratior. by all the partners was all the more necessary in view 
of the fact that to the particular misconduct rnenituled in the charge sheet, 
heing a misconduct under Standing Order No. 17 ( 1 ) ( e ) , dismissal is not the only 
punisment to be awarded . Clause (i ) of Standing Order No . 17 provides that 
@ worlman 1 : y be suspended or fined or his increment may be stopped or he 
may be demoted or dismissed without notice , if he is found to be guilty of mis 
conduct . The partners should have considered the desirability of passing a 
lesser purishment than dismissal . The tone of the letters of dismissal clearly 
Indicate that the partners were too eager to grasp the opportunity of dismissing 
the concerned workmen . Materials on record show that the relation between 
the management and the workmen was far from happy . The management ought 
to have taken extra precautio in dismming the concerned wcrkmen . 


18 . The order of dismissal appears to be bad on another ground . Clause ( li ) 
of Standing Order No. 17 requires that the approval of the owner , agent or the 
Chief Mining Erzineer of the employer or a person holding similar position 
shall be obtained before imposing the punishment of dismissal. In this case both 
the letters of dismissal were issued by D . P . Lodha. He is only one of the joint 
owners . There is nothing on record to show that the dismissal of the concerned 
workmen was approved by the other partners too . Then again , from the depo 
sition of Shiva Kumar Lodha it transpires that it is he who looks after the man 
ngement of the colliery as a partner of the concern . The letter of dismissal, in 
any event, should have been issued by him as the managing partner and not hy 
D . P . Lodha . Then it could perhaps have been seid that the dismissal had the 
Approval of a rerson holding a nosition similar to that of an agent or the Chief 
Mining Engineer. However , in this case the letters of dismissal wore issued by 
D . P . Lodha on behalf of the owners, but these two lotters do not show that 
dismissal had the approval of all the partners. The order of dismissal is liable 
to be set aside on that ground also . 


19 . The next question for consideration is whether the management of Dhati 
sar Colliery is justified in not making payment of profit sharing bonus to their 
worlmen for the accounting period 1966 - 1967; if not, to what relief they are 
entitled . Shiva Kumar Lodha, MW2 has admitted in his cross -examination that 
the workmen are entitled to get the profit sharing bonus for the year 1966 -67 
as per law . But his case is that the profit sharing bonus for the accounting year 
1966 -67 is to be paid by Indra Kumar Agarwalla , if not already pald .

20 . On the question of profit sharing bonus the employer s case is follows. 
From 1 . 63 to June, 1967 the Dharsar Colliery wus und - r the inanagement and

agcreat took over charge of the colliery from the 3rd July , 1967. Before the

21. The further case of the employers is that under the terms of the agree 
ment between Indra Kumar Agarwalla and the present management, it was 
Agarwalla who was responsible for the payment of all dues including profit shar 
ing bonus to the workmen In their written statement they nave stated that 
Indra Kumar Agarwalla is & nccessary party for the proper and effective adjudi 
calion of the dispute regarding the payment of profit sharing bonus for the 
accounting year 1966-67. 

22 . In exercise of the powers conferred by sub - section 3 (b ) of section 18 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act , 1917 my predecessor - in - office pusad an order on the 3rd 
April, 1970 impleading Indra Kumar Agarwalla as a party to the procecding . 
Suallicory notice was issued to him asking him to file his written statement. But 
intra Kumar Agarwalla has neither entered appearancc nor filed his written 
statouent. 

23. The memorandum oſ agrcement deved the 2nd September , 1963 between 
Inara Kuraar Agarwalla and the Lodhas, who constitute the present manage 
nient has been fied by the Lodhas, and it has been marked as Ext , M13 . The 
Truhus are toe hoiders of the leaselioid interest in the collicly. By an agree. 
ment dated the 7til June, 1955 the Lodhas appointed Basanta Kumar Agarwalla 
kad thcir Agent to work the colliery . The said agrecment having been termi 
nated by tual consent before the expiry of the term the Lodhas entered into 
a iresh agreement on the 2nd September , 1863 with Indra Kumar Agarwalla for 
toe management and working of the colliery ( Ext. M13 ) . Clause 6 of M13 says 
that during the subsistence of the agreement, Indra Kumar Agarwalla describ 
ed as the Agent shall have full charge and control of the mines , quatries, in 
clines etc . of the colliery . Clause 13 provides that Indra Kumar Agarwalla as 
Agent shall employ such staff and employees and labour as are required for the 
efficient working of the mine, and that the Agent shall be fully responsible for 
11e salary and remuneration and for payment of their provident fund , bonus and 
other dues , under clause 15 of the Ext. M13 the Lodhas are cntitled to a share 
of profit at the rate of 8 per cent of the net sale proceeds of the coal despatched 
subject to a minimum of Rs. 18000 per year . Clause 25 gives the Agent option 
to terminate the agency by giving six months notice but precludes the Lodhas 
from terminating the agency before the 1st day of September, 1980 . 

24 . Indra Kumar Agarwalla in exercise of the option given to him by clause 
25 gave notice on the 17th December, 1966 asking the Lodhas to take posses 
sion of the colliery on and from the 1st July , 1967. The contract of agency thus 
c :ure to an end on the 30th June, 1967 . It is , therefore . clear that in the account 
111 : ycar 1966 -67 Indra Kumar Agarwalla was in absolute control of the colliery , 
He was, therefore , the employer within the meaning of clause ( 14 ) of sec . 2 of 
the Payment of Bonus Act, 1965. Again under clause 13 of the agency agrce 
ment Indra Kumar Agarwalla was to employ such staff and labour as were re 
quired for the efficient management of the colliery and he was responsible for 
paying them their salaries, wages, bonus etc . 

25 . From the judgment delivered on the 7th August , 1968 (Ext. M8) in T . R . 
Case No. 389 of 1968 C . W . W . Case No. 71 of 1967 it appears that Indra Kumar 
Agarwalla was found guilty of an offence under section 73 of the Mines Act ag 
the owner and was sentenced to pay a fine of Rs. 125 . Three out of the four 
Lodhas were also tried along with Indra Kumar Agarwalla but thev were acquit 
1 - 0 Another judgment delivered on the 7th August, 1968 ( Ext. M8 ) shows that 
though Indra Kumar Agarwalla was tried along with three of the Lodhas for 
the contravention of sec. 73 of the Mines Act, he alone was found guilty and 
convicted as owner and the Lodhas were acquitted . The three Lodhas were 
tried for the contravention of section 9 ( a ) and ( b ) of the Coal Mines Provident 
Fund and Bonus Scheme Act, 1948 , but they were acquitted on the ground that 
they did not constitute the owner within the meaning of that Act. The judg 
ment in that case was delivered on the 17th May, 1969 (Ext . M9) . 

26 . In paragraph 3 of the letter dated the 17th October , 1966 (Ext. M14 )

the collierv business from him on the 1st July , 1967, subject to and in accordance 
with the relevant clauses of the Agreement, dated the 2nd September , 1963 (Ext .

M10 ) . It has been stated above that under the said Agreement Indra Kumar 
Agarwalla was responsible and liable for the salary and remuneration and for

employ and discharge the employees and staff including the Manager 

27. Thc Lodhas wrote a letter to Indra Kumar Agarwalla on the 18th Feb . 
ruary , 1967 ( Ext. 1715 ) . Itein ( d ) of the said letter runs as follows: " Arrange 
to terminate the services of all employees and workmen appointed by you at 
the colliery giving them timely notice of termination of their services including 
the lines Manager. You should , therefore, also arrange to pay them all their 
due upto the 30th June, 1967, including salary, dearness allowance , provident 
fund contribution , bonus, compensation under the Workmen s Compensation Act 
etc , etc ," Ext. M16 is the letter written in reply by Indra Kumar Agarwalla 
on the 6th Junc, 1967, clause ( b ) of which reads as follows: " As far itcm ( d ) , 
under the terms of the above agreement, it is not obligatory on my part to 
terminate the services of the employees, workmen and the Manager in service 
at the colliery . I am , however , arranging to pay all their dues on account of 
their salary , dearness allowances , etc . upto and including the 30th June, 1967. 
From the 1st July , 1967, payment of salary etc . of all the coitiery staff and Mines 
Managcr and workmen will be yours ," The word " etc ." after " dearness allow 
ance" makes it clear that Indra Kumar Agarwalla undertook to pay frofit sharing 
bonus as well. 

28 . Apart from the undertaking given by Indra Kumar Agarwalla in his let 
ter dated the 6th June, 1967. under the law too he alone is bound to pay the 
profits sharing bonus. Section 8 of the Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 provides 
that every employee shall be entitled to be paid by his employer in a account 
ing ycar, bonus, in accordance with the provisions of this Act , provided he has 
worked in the establishment for not less than thirty working days in that year , 
The words in an accounting year " qualify the words " his employer ." Section 
8 rcally means that an employee is entitled to be paid bonus by his employer in 
an accounting year. Section 19 says that all amounts payable to an en .ployee 
by way of bonus shall be paid in cash by his employer when there is no dispute 
pending under section 22 within a period of eight months from the close of the 
accounting year. The employec must look to his cmployer in the accounting 
year for the payment of the bonus in respect of the accounting year . 
It is , therefore, clear that Indra Kumar Agarwalla was responsible for the pay 
mont of the bonus duc for the accounting year 1906 - 1967 . because he was the 
employer within the mcaning of the Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 . There is little 
substance in the argument of Mr. Burman appearing on behalf of workmen that 
the Lodias are liable to pay the profit sharing bonus as under clause 15 of the 
Agreement dated the 2170 September , 1963 tey were entitled to claini 3 share 
of the profit . Clouse 15 rcally provides for consideration for the right given to 
Indra Kumar Agarwalla to remain in exclusive possession and control ci the 
collirry during the subsistence of the agreement. Clause 15 mercly lays down 
the method of calculating the amount to be paid to the Lodhas subicct to a 
guaranteed minimum of Rs. 18000 per annum . Clause , 15 hors not the effect of 
making the Lodhas partners of Indra Kumar Agarwalla . The Lodhas cannot 
be made liable under clause 15 of the Agreement. The Lodhas cannot be regard 
ed as the successor - in - interest of Indra Kumar Agarwalla ; therefore, even under 
the general law they cannot be made liable for the payment of the profit sharing 
bonus for the accounting year 1966 -67. 

29 . I accordingly award as follows: So far as item ( i ) of the Schedule is 
concerned the management of Dhansar Colllery was rnt fustified in dismissing 
Shri Jago Gone und Kishun Singh with effcct from 14th August . 1968 : and the 
two concerned workmen , Kishun Singh and Jago Gope are entitled to be re 
instated in their seryice with effect from the 14th August. 1968 with back wages 
from that date after deducting the amount, if any. received hv them from the 
employers on account of wages or other benefit in the mean time. 

As to item ( ii) of the Schedule , the management of Dhansar Colliery was not 
justified in not making payment of profit sharing bonus to the work 
men for the accounting year 1966 - 67 ; and the workmen are entitled to claim 
their profit sharing bonus for the said accounting year from Indra Kumis Agar 
walla who managed the Colliery in the said accounting vet and not from the 
prerent management who took over charge on and from the 1st July , 1967 .

az is extended by mutual agreement between the parties in wr. tirg . 

2 . On 10th December , 1970 both the parties were requested to submit their 
written stalenient to me by 31st December, 1970 with simultaneously / endorsing 
a copy to the opposite party . The employers and the Union both prayed for

subsequertly while the employers subinitted their written istement on 21st J2ll 
ary, 1971 , the Union Aled no statement. The dispute came up for hearing at

4 . While presenting the case of the employers, Shri Das Gupta stated inter alia 
that Shri Shyamdeo Singh was appointed in the Colliery in 1948 . On 10th Novem 
ber , 1969 the workman was on duty from 8 A . M . to 4 P . M . At about 3 . 30 P . M . 
on ihat day , while the workman was on duty at the Doct of the Colliery , he 
neglected his duty inasmuch as some outsiders were taking coal from the Depot 
without any authorised slips. Shri Budhan Mahato , Empty Wazon Checking 
mazdoor who was also on duty observed that the workman was kecping silent and 
was not checking the unauthorised persons taking coal, Shri Mahato , therefore , 
forced the unauthorised persons to unload the coal and he complained to another 
Guar :1 , Shri Chutar Singh about the incident when the wurkman got arnoyed and 
started abusing Shri Mahato . Thereafter, the workman approached Shri Mahato 
with a stick and all of a sudden struck him with the same. There were other 
persons who were witness to the incident and they separated Shri Mahato from 
the workmen . Shri Mahato was also seen weeping by some other workmen , The 
workman was chargeshceted on 12th November, 1969 for abusing and assaulting 
Shrl Budhan Malato during the duty hours. Shri Das Gupta stated thut the 
chargesheet was given for misconduct under Clause 2 ( 5 ) of the certilled Standing 
Orders inpplicable to the establishment. The Labour Officer of the Colliery . Shri 
A . K . Tarafdar was appointed as an Enquiry Oncer, wird after serving due notices 
held the enquiry on 16th May, 1970 . During the enquiry , 5 prosecution witnesses 
deposed evidences before the Enquiry Oficer . These included Shri Budhan 
Mahato , who was abused and assaulted , one Khalasi, one Fitter helper , one Loading 
Clerk and the Medical Officer , who had treated Shri Budhan Mahato for his injury . 
The delinqucnt produced only one witness viz . Shri Chutar Singh , Guard . Shri 
Das Gupto maintained that the delinquent was given full opportunity to cross 
examine the prosecution witnesses and the delinquent actually cross examined 
4 prosecution witnesses and declined to cross examine PW . 2 . Shri Sabrati Mia , 
S . P , Khalasi, The Enquiry Officer submitted his report on 20th May , 1970 
holding that the charge framed against the delinquent were proved beyond all 
reasonable doubts . Thereafter , the Manager of the Colliery on 22nd May, 1970 
recommended dismissal of the workman to the then Deputy Superintendent and 
also the Agent of the Poniati Group . On 13th July , 1970 , the Agent of the Colliery 
ordered dismissal of the workman and Anally on 16th July , 1970 . the dismissal 
order was served on Shri Shyamdeo Singh . Summing up his case , Shri Das Gupta 
stated that the workman was in the habit of abusing. guarrelling and actually 
fighting with hls co -workmen and supcriors as would be obvious from his service 
records. The employers case was therefore , that the dismissal of the workman is 
Justified , legal, bonafled and in accordance with the approved Standing Orders , 
Shri Das Gupta , therefore, prayed to give award accordingly . 

5 . Shrl . K . Rudra , while presenting the case on behalf of the workman , 
stated in brief, that the chargęsheet given to the workman was vague ets it did not 
specify the time of the incident and did not elaborate the type of abuse hurled 
on Shri Mahato . Shri Rudra further maintained that the findings of the Enquiry 
Omcer were perverse and the charge as contained in the chargesheet was not at 
all proved . According to the Union , as the enquiry was not thorough and proper 
and as the charge was not proved , the dismissal of the workman was wrongful 
and illegal, Summing up his arguments . Shri Rudra prayed that in ylew of the 
aforesaid position the dismissal of Shri Shyamdeo Singh was not justified and 
hence the workman deserves relastatement. 

8 . The Union has built up its case mainly on three grounds - the vagueness of 
the chargesheet, blased and perverse enquiry proceedings and want of conclusivo 
proof of the charge of abuse and assorult . Let the factual position in regard to 
these points be now analysed . Shri Shvamdeo Singh was on duty from 8AM , 
to 4 P . M . on 10th November, 1969 near the Depot is a fact , which is not disputed . 
The chargesheet No . 64 served on the workman on : 2th Noverabor, 1969 at the 


BEC . 3 (ii ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24 , 1971 /VAISAKHA 4 , 1893 


2085 


very beginning gives the date and time of incident as “ 10th November, 1969 at 
4 P . M ." . Therefore, the argument of Shri Rudra that the chargesheat is vague 
in regard to date and time of the incident, hence falls to ground . A to the 
content of the chargesheet the relevant entry is : 
" For abusing and assaulting Shri Budhan Mahato , Empty wagon checking 

mazdoor when he protested against robbing of coal by others from 
the depot during the duty hours. Thus you have v . olated Standing 

Order No. 27 (5 ) ” . 
Clause 27 (5 ) of the certified Standing Orders enumerates the following acts 
as misconduct : 

“ 27 ( 5 ) Drunkenness, fighting , riotous or disorderly or indecent behaviour " . 
7 . It is thus seen that while the Standing Orders makes mention in general of 
certain acts, the chagesheet particulars the misconduct viz " abuse and 
" assault” . The argument of the Union about the vagueness of the chargesheet 
thius , has no strength . 

8 . While dealing with the manner in which the enquiry Proceedings were 
conducted , Shri Rudra with a forceful manner attempted to argue that the enquiry 
does not prove ihe cause leading to the resultant alleged abuse and assault . As 
the chargesheet was not foi the neglecti in duty on the part of the worknian 
( checking unauthorised robbing of coal etc . ) , there does not appear to be any 
reason, why this aspect should have been focussed during the enquiry . It is 
interesting to note that during the cross -examination of the P . W I, Shri Budhan 
Mahats , the delinquent has posed questions that while he was the only Guardi at 
the Depot how was it possible for him to see the whole depot area . This itself 
proves that the delirojeni does not deny the happening of the robbing of the coal 
by unauthorised rersons. Anyway, there is nothing to prove that the principles 
of natural justice were not followed by the Enquiry Ollicer , while hoiding the 
enquiry on 16th May , 1970 . The rules of natural justice require that a party 
should have the opportur: ity of adducing of relevant evidence on which he relies . 
that the evidence of the opponent should be taken in his presence, and that he 
should be given the opportunity of cross - examining the witnesses examined by 
that party , and that no materials should be relied on against him without his 
being given an opportunity of explaining them in the instant case a minute 
scrutiny of the enquiry papers revealed that all these requirements have been 
followed properly . I do not find any reason in not relying on the contentions of 
the employers in regard to the manner in which the enquiry was conducted . 

9 . Let me now examine the third and the final argument putforth by Shri 
Rudra on behalf of the workman in regard to the proving of the charge levelled 
against the workman . The charge is mainly for abusing and assai tin , Shri 
Budhan Mahatc . Durirg the entire enquiry proceedings, the workman did not 
deny his abusing and assaulting Shri Budhan Mahato . During the encuiry . Shri 
Budhan Mahato has deposed at length how he was abused and assaulted by the 
workman . Inimediately after the incident, Shri Mahato went to his quarters and 
as the assault made by stick on him was giving lot of pain , he had to go to the 
Colliery Medical Officer for treatment. Dr. M . S . Saha , who was P . W , 5 has stated 
during the enquiry that Shri Budhan Mahato was treated at the Colliery Dispen 
sary on 10th November , 1969 and the outdoor patient register records the entry 
“ pain and swelling - ieft thigh " against the entry of Shri Budhan Mahato . Shri 
Ashoke Chakraborty , P . . 3 had deposed during the enquiry that he was a witness 
to the incident of assaiit on Shri Mahato by Shri Shyamcleo Singh . He actually 
intervened in the incident and separated the two. About the abuse, it has been 
stated during the enquiry by Shri Mahato that the workman had hurled abuses 
at him viz Badmash etc. Thus, there is enough evidence proving the charge of 
abuse and assault by the workman . 

10 . Having therefore carefully considered the facts of the case and the evidences 
adduced during the enquiry as revealed from the enquiry papers, and other mate 
rials placed befcre me by the parties and further having considered the pleadings 
during hearing of the case and in view of the aforesaid facts , I hold that the 
action of the management in dismissing Shri Shyamdeo Singh , Decot Guard from 
16th July , 1970 was justified . In view of this the question of any relief to the 
workman does not arise . This is my AWARD .

excellent cooperation given to me during the course of arbitration hearing

AGREEMENT 

Short recital of the case 
Shri Kapildeo Mahto , working as pump khalasi was stopped from work with 
effect from 8th June, 1970. He remained out of employment till 241h Junc, 1970 . 
An Industrial dispute for non employment of the workman and wages for the period 
of idleness was raised by the union which ended in failure in conciliation proceed 
Ing before the A . L , C . ( C ) Dhanbad - 1. The Government of India in the Ministry 
of Labour and Employment referred this dispute for adjudication to the Certral 
Government Industrial Tribunal No . 2 at Dhanbad vide notification No. 2 / 118 / 70 
LRII dated 6th October , 1970 . 

After reference of this dispute to the Tribunal the parties set and resolved this. 
difference among themselves and have settled the differences on the following 
terms. 

Terms of Agreement 
1. The management agrees to pay half wages to the workmen Kapildeo 

Mahto for the period 8th June, 1970, to 24th June, 1970 , in presence 

of the representatives of the Panchayat . 
2 . That the continuity of scrvice of Sri Kapildeo Mahto shall not be affected 

any way due to break in his service due to stoppage of work .

BEFORE THE PRESIDING OFFICER. CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL

Employers in relation to M / s . Khas Jeenagora Coal Co . ( P ) Ltd . 

AND 
Their Workmen . 

REFERENCE No. 70 OF 1968 
After the reference of this dispute for adjudication the management represen 
tative and the Union representative sat and discussed to resolve the dispute in the 
interest of mutual good relation and industrial peace. As a result of this mutual 
discussions parties have arrived at a settlement. 

It is requested that this tribunal be pleased to give award in terms of this 
settlement. 

Terms of Settlement 
1 . That the management have paid full retrenchment compensation to all 
workmen as laid down in Section 25F of the I. D . Act , except Serial Nos . 12 , 20 , 
21 , 34, 40 , 41, 58 and 64 i.e . Şri J . C . Dasandhu , Sri Awadhesh Pandey, Sri Shyam 
Sunder Singh , Sambhu Mallik , Gopi Hazzam , Hemraj Barhi, Mahatam Saw and 
Nefar Rabidas. 

2. That Faudi Bhuiya Şurface Trammer serial No. 83 is already dead . The 
question of his reinstatement does not arise . 

3. That the management have re - employed serials Nos. 1, 2 , 3 , 4 and 5 i.e . Sri 
B . B . Singh , Sri M . L . Nunia , Sri S . K . Singh , Sri B . N . Mahata , and Sri B . K . 
Sural and they have also been paid full compensation as laid down under Section 
25F of the I. D . Act . 

4 . That the management though have paid full retrenchment compensation as 
laid down under Section , 25F of the Act, undertake to re - employ them in the 
group of Collicries owned by this management excepting Serial Nos , 1 , 2 , 3 , 4 , 5 ag 
they have already been employed and Serial No. 83 who has died . 

5 . That the management and the Union undertake to inform the workmen to 
join their duties by the 15th day from the receipt of the award . 

6 . That, if any workmen fails to report for duty by the 15th day from the 
J eceipt of the award even after intimation shall forfiet his claim for re - employment, 

7. That serial No. 12 , 20 , 21, 34 , 40 , 41, 58 & 64 namely Sri J. C . Dasandhi, Sri 
Awadhesh Pandey , Sri Shyam Sundar Singh , Sri Sambhu Mallik , Sri Gopi Hazzam 
Sri Hem Raj Barhi, Sri Mahatam Saw , and Sri Nefar Rabidas shall be reinstated 
and shall be paid compensation as has been paid to other workmen in the list of 
reference . 

8 . That continuity of service shall be given to all the workmen who resume thelr 
duty within the prescribed time limit. 

This agreement as stated above settles the claims of all workmen mentioned 
in the list of the reference.

Shri K , Saran , Regional Labour Commissioner ( Central) , Asansol, Arbitrator, in

the Burrakur Coal Company Limited , Post Office Ukhra, District Burdwan and 
their workmen represented by Colliery Mazdoor Sabha ( AITUC ) . GT Road , 
Asansol, which was received by the Central Government on the 5th April, 1971.

AWARD 


· The Central Government having received a written agreement in pursuance 
of the provisions of sub - section ( 1 ) of Section 10A of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947 ) between the management of Bankola Colliery of Ms. The 
Burrakur Coal Co . Ltd ., P . O . Ukhra , Dist. Burdwan (hereinafter referred to as 
the management) and their workman represented by the Colliery Mazdoor Sabha 
( AITUC ) , GT, Road , Asansol (hereinafter referred to as the union ) referring 
the industrial dispute between them , the specific matters in dispute being as 
detailed below to my arbitration , and the Central Government being of the opi 
nion that the Industrial dispute referred to above existed between the manage 
ment and the union , ordered publication of the said arbitration agreement in the 
Gazette of India , Part II , Section 3 , sub - section ( ii) under its order No. 8 / 118170 
LR . II , dated the 29th August, 1970 : 
" Specific matters in dispute : 
What should be the nucleus (permanent) strength of the Wagon Loaders 

of Bankola Colliery of Ms. Burrakur Coal Co. Ltd ., P . O . Ukhra , 

Dist . Burdwan .” 
2. The General Secretary of the Colliery Mazdoor Sabha (AITUC ), Asansol 
was requested under my letter No. E . 1 /10 ( 2 ) 170 , dated 22nd July , 1970 to submit 
written statement on behalf of the workmen endorsing a copy thereof to the 
management under intimation to me and simultaneously the Manager , Bankola 
Colliery was requested to submit written statement on behalf of the inanage 
ment and also rejoinder , if any , on the written statement of the workmen under 
my letter No. E . 1 / 10 ( 2 ) |70 , dated 22nd July , 1970 , endorsing a copy thereof to 
the union . The union had submitted written statement on 13th August, 1970 , 
which was received by me on 13th August , 1970 . The management had submitted 
written statement on 27th August, 1970 which was received by me on 27th 
August, 1970 . In forwarding a copy of the written statement, dated 27th August, 
1970 of the management to the union under my letter No. E 1110 ( 2 ) |70 , dated 
20th August , 1970 , the union was requested to submit rejoinder on the written 
statement of the management so as to reach me latest by th September , 1970 . 
The union submitted rejoinder on 23rd November, 1970 which was received by 
me on 26th November , 1970 . Thereafter the matter came up for hearing on 
5th December, 1970 . On 5th December, 1970 Shri J . Sharan , Personnel Officer 
of the management was present on behalf of the management and Shri Sunil 
Sen , Organising Secretary , Colliery Mazdoor Sabha was present on behalf of 
the workmen . The representative of the union Aled a petition requesting there 
in to call upon the management to produce certain documents which the repre 
sentative of the management assured to produce, After preliminary discussions 
the hearing was adjourned with the consent of the representatives of both 
parties. I Axed up hearing in the matter on 28th December , 1970 but in the mean 
time the representative of the management Aled petition dated 21st December

1970 And requested to adjourn the hcaring . With the consent of the 
parties the hearing was adjourned to be held in my oflice on 5th January , 1971, 
On 5th January , 1971 Shri M . K . Multherjee , Advocate , Asansol and Shri J , Sha 
r an , Personnel Officer were present on behalf of the management. Shri Sunil 
Sen , Organising Secretary, Colliery Mazdoor Sabha , Asansol was present on be 
hall of the workmen , On that date the management submitted a rejoinder , dated 
5th January , 1971 to the written statement of the union and a copy of the same 
was handed over to the representative of the union . After preliminary hearing 
I called upon the representative of the management to submit monthly state 
ment showing the number of ordinary wagons and box wagons indicating their 
Capacity , supplied on cach day of the month , showing the number of such wagons 
loaded nicchanically as well as manually each day separately for the 
months of January , 1970 to December, 1970 . After this , with the con 
sent of the parties the hearing was adjourned to be held in my office on 14th 
January , 1971 . On 14th January , 1971 Shri J . Sharan , Personnel Officer was pre 
sent on behalf of the management and Shri Sunil Sen , Organising Secretary was 
present on bchalf of the workmen . On behalf of the management 35 papers 
documents were Aled which with the consent of the representative of the work 
men were marked Exis. M . l (a ) to M , 1 ( d ) , M . 2 ( a ) to M . 2 ( h ), Ext. 3 , Ext. 4 ( a ) to 
4 ( h ) , Ext. : 5 ( ) to 5 ( C ) , Ext. 6 ( 2 ) 10 6 ( c ) , Ext , 7 ( a ) to Ext. 7 ( d ) and Ext. 8 ( a ) to 
Ext . 8 ( a ) . The representative of the workmen , however , did not file any docu 
mentary evidence . On behalf of the management Shri S . K . Şingh , Superinten 
dent (MW . 1 ) was produced as witness. He was examined and cross - examined . 
On behalf of workmen ane witness namely Shri Bancha Mondol (WW . 1 ) was 
produced . He was examined and cross - examined . In the meantime the parties 
entered into an agreement agreeing therein that I might give my award in the 
instant industrial dispute latest by 31st March , 1971. Finally , I heard the argu 
ments of both the partics on 22nd January , 1971. 


3 . The case of the union as made out in their written statement is in brief 
that large number of wagon loaders had been engaged by the management for 
long time and they were not being paid wages directly and that their attendances 
were not marked properly and they were not paid bonus and other benefits ; that 
the management had resorted to unfair labour practices in as much as they 
had kept them as temporary and changed their names from time to time to 
deprive them of their legitimate legal dues etc. ; that the wagon loaders had been 
agitating since long for their proper marking of attendances , direct payment and 
also for increase in the number of permanent workmen ; that as a result of pro 
longed agitation , the management agreed to make direct payment since January, 
1970 ; that the average monthly despatch figure of coal loaded into wagons by 
"hand loading was to the tune of 40, 000 tonnes per month ; that in view of the 
same the number of permanent wagon loaders was very poor but inspite of the 
agitation by the wagon loaders tho management remained adamant and did not 
increase the number of permanent wagon loaders and on the contrary the manage . 
mcnt adopted the policy of improper distribution of wagons to the temporary 
wagon loaders with a view to harassing them ; that the manageinent was bound 
to increase the number of permancnt wagon loaders as per the direction of the 
law on the basis of average monthly despatch figure with retrospective effect. 
Finally the union demanded that all the 125 wagon loaders enlisted in its written 
statenient be made permanent with retrospective effect. In their rejoinder to 
the written statement submitted by the management, the union submitted that 
the monthly despatch of coal through wagon loading was to the tune of 40 , 000 
tonnes but denied that the joy loader and conveyor belt could load 28 , 000 tonnes 
per month and also denied that hand loading on an average in a month was to 
the tune of 12 , 000 tonnes as contended by the management. It was further assert 
ed by the union that the average hand loading was 32 ,000 tonnes per month and 
accordingly the nucleus permanent strength of wagon loaders would be 255 against 
which the nucleus permanent strength of wagon loaders in the colliery was only 
110 and it was demanded that all the wagon loaders enlisted in its written state 
ment ibe directed to be declared as permanent with retrospective effect . 


4 . The case of the management as made out in its written statement in brief 
is that the average despatch of coal through wagon loading is to the tune of 
40 ,000 tonnes per month ; that out of these total despatch , the conveyor belt and 
joy loader have the capacity to load to the tune of 28, 000 tonnes per inonth cal 
culated on the basis of 26 working days in a month or in other words 20 box 
wagons per day equivalent to 11,00 tonnes per day ; that the average loading of 
coal by wagon loaders through manual loading comes to the tune of about 12 ,000 
tonnes per month ; that on an average a wagon loader is supposed to load 4 . 5
