ton per day on the basis of the recommendations of the Central Wage Board for 
the Coal Mining Industry ; that on this basis the total requirement of wagon 
loaders comes to 102 wagon loaders but as against this the management has per 
manent nucleus strength of 116 , wagon loaders ; that the allegations of the union 
as incorporated in its written statement, apart from being artificial are irrelevant 
in respect of the issue under reference. Therefore, the management prayed that 
the strength of the permanent wagon loaders was more than adequate . The 
management in its rejoinder to the written siatement submitted by the Collicry 
Mazdoor Sabha subinitted further that the management had completely abolished 
the contract labour system in respect of coal loading and unloading and there 
fore determination and / or review of nucleus strength of the wagon loaders in 
accordance with the agrcement adopted by the Dave Court of Enquiry was 
neither legally permissible nor could it be the subject matter for any arbitration . 
Moreover , without prejudice to the above contention the management contended . 
that during the procecding three months viz ., October, 1970 , November , 1970 
and December , 1970 the average monthly despatch of coal came to 36 ,210 metric 
tonnes and keeping in view the capacity of the conveyor belt and joy loader the 
number of wagon loaders required as nucleus strength came to about 66 cal 
culated on the basis of work load of 4 .5 tonnes . The capacity of wagon loaders 
of the management was to load much more than 4 . 5 tonnes per day and , therefore , 
after taking the same into consideration , the nucleus strength would be even 
less than 66 . The management denied the contention of the union that the aver 
age loading by hand woked out to 32,000 tonnes per month . In order to avoid 
laying off of permanent wagon loaders when supply position of wagons was poor 
or when there was break down of machineries, the machine loading was either 
suspended or reduced to provide employment to all the permanent wagon loaders . 

5 . During the course of hearing it was admitted by both parties that the 
system of engaging wagon loaders through contractors was abolished in Bankola 
Colliery of the management since January , 1970 . It was , therefore , prcsscd very 
much by Shri M . K , Mukherjee, advocate , learned representative of the manage 
ment that in accordance with the report of the Central Government Court of 
Enquiry ( Coal Mining Industry ) , Calcutta , in the industrial dispute between the 
employers in relation to coal mining industry and their workmen over the aboli 
tion of the contract labour system in coal mining industry the management was 
required to maintain a nucleus strength of wagon loaders ONY when thc work of 
coal loading and unloading was done through the contractors . As the managc 
ment in the instant case had abolished the contract labour system with regard 
to the loading and unloading of coal since January , 1970 and continued to engage 
all wagon loaders directly , there was absolutely no point in my determining the 
strength of nucleus of wagon loaders . I reproduce below para 27 of the report 
of the said court of enquiry which reads as under : 
" 27. The fourth category is coal loading and unloading. The agreement on 

this category contains a proviso that the principal employer shall 
engage a nucleus of wagon and truck loaders to whom regular work 
can be guarantecd the number of such nucleus to be reviewed 
quarterly . I am told that the position of wagon supply is not regular 
and sometimes extra work of loading and unloading may be required 
to be done, and that such work would be of temporary nature 
and hence it is said that it may be allowed to be done through con 
tractors . This is , however , likely to load to mal- practices . For ins 
tance an employer may engage a nucleus of loaders at much less 
than his normal and usual requirements and thus evade doing the 
work departmentally . I am , however, accepting the agreement only 

because it is agreed that it would be reviewed later on ." 
I also reproduce below relevant extracts of the agreement dated the 30th 
October , 1961 between the representatives of the coal industry and their work 
men which formed part of the said report : 
" 2 . It is further agreed that all processes directly connected with the rais 

ing and despatch of coal and manufacture and despatch of coke shall 
be the direct responsibility of the principal employer and all workers 
engaged therein shall be the employees of the principal employer 
except in the following categories : 


( iv ) Coal loading and unloading provided that the Principal Employer 

shall engage a nucleus of wagon loaders to whom regular work can 
be guranteed ; the number of such nucleus to be reviewed quarterly ," 


SEC. 3 ( ii )] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24 , 1971 / VAISAKHA 4, 1893 


2099 


There is force in the argument of Shri Mukherjee in this regard , but at the same 
time the specific matters in dispute before me which is also reproduced below 
cannot be lost sight of : 

" Specific matters in dispute : 
What should be the nucleus ( permanent ) strength of the wagon loaders of 

Bankola Colliery of M / s . Burrakur Coal Co. Ltd ., P . O . Ukhra , Distt. 

Burdwan ." 
In the terms of reference there is no mention of the report of the court of enquiry 
referred to above, on the contrary the meaning of the word , nucleus which the 
parties had in their minds while referring the dispute to my arbitration has been 
precisely made clear by inserting the word, permanent in bracket after the word , 
nucleus . The terms of reference in the arbitration agreement in question clearly 
goes to show that the parties want me to determine the permanent strength of 
the wagon loaders in Bankola Colliery of the manugement or in other words I am 
required to determine the strength of the permanent wagon loaders in the colliery . 

6 . With regard to the basis on which the strength of the permanent wagon 
loaders should be determined , divergent views have been placed before me by 
the learned representatives of both partis , Shri Sunil Sen learned representative 
of the workmen argued before me that the strength of the permanent wagon 
loaders should be determined on the basis of average quantity of coal actually 
loaded into wagons at the colliery through hand loading, He further stated that 
Exts . M . I ( a ) to M . I ( d ) which are the statements submitted by the managements 
showing the number of ordinary wagons loaded manually and through machines 
and the number of box wagons loaded manually and through machines each day 
for the months of January , 1970 to December , 1970 show that 32,567 tonnes of 
coal were loaded manually on an average in a month or in other words about 20 
box wagons were required to be loaded manually on an average each day . Thus 
according to him the strength of permanent wagon loaders came to 243. As 
against this the learned representative of the management argued that the figures 
of quantity of coal actually loaded into wagons manually during the period 
January , 1970 to December , 1970 should not be taken as criteria for determining 
the strength of the permanent wagon loaders in as much as the wagon loaders 
forcibly did not allow thc management to utilise the full capacity of two machines 
in operation in loąding coal into wagons and on the contrary the wagon loaders 
used to forcibly load the coal into wagons manually . Moreover , whenever 
number of wagons allotted to the colliery was inadequate to the management 
either stopped mechanical loading altogether or reduced the number of wagons to 
be loaded mechanically and diverted wagons for manual loading in order to pro 
vide work to the wagon loaders with a view to avoiding labour unrest . Besides, the 
wagons were diverted for manual loading some times because of breakdown 
in machines and some times on account of defects in the brakes of the wagons 
rendering them unsuitable for mechanical loading. In this connection the manage 
ment placed reliance on the warning letters issued to various gang heads of 
wagon loaders vide Exts , M . ( 2a ) to M . 2 ( h ) , M . 4 ( a ) to M .4 ( h ) as well as on the 
statement of Shri S , K , Singh , Superintendent (MW . I ) . The exhibits referred 
to above do indicate that the wagon loaders had forcibly loaded the wagons which 
were meant for mechanical loading . I also reproduce the relevant extracts of 
the statement of Shri S . K . Singh (MW . I) : -- 


At Bankola Colliery loading of coal into wagons is being done mechanically 
a s well as munually . There are two machines which are in use for the purpose 
of loading coal into wagons. In addition to these two machines there is one more 
machine but it is lying idle for the present but it can be used for that purpose . 
One of the machines which is in use has the capacity of loading of over 550 
tonnes per day or in other words ten box wagons. Rest of the two machines has 
the capacity of loading more than ten box wagons per day each . In normal 
circumstances i.e . when there is no breakdown and the supply of wagon is ade 
quate twenty box wagons have been loaded in one day . As per the record we 
had loaded 19 - 1 / 4 box wagons on 18th July , 1970 , 20 - 2 / 3 box wagons on 23rd July , 
1970 . 19 - 1 / 2 box wagons on 30th July , 1970 . The full capacity of the machines for 
loading wagons cannot be achieved on all the days because of (i ) irregular sup 
ply of wagons, ( ii) breakdown in the machineries , ( iii) forcible loading of wagons 
by casual wagon loaders and ( iv ) defective brakes in the box wagons, On 
some days when the number of wagons supplied is less , we provide the wagons 
to the permanent wagon loaders by stopping mechanical loading to avoid laying 
off of permanent wagon loaders. If we put into commission the third machine 
which is at present idle we will have to reduce the number of permanent wagon 
loaders . It will not be possible to utilise such of the wagons which have defective 


2100 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24, 1971 / VAISAKHA 4 , 1893 [PART II — 


brakes for mechanical loading because for the mechanical loading the movement 
of the wagon is to be precisely controlled lest they move away from the mecha 
nical loading point. That is why those defective wagons are utilised only for 
manual loading. 


Cross - examination by Shri Sunil Sen ,Organising Secretary . 


It is also correct that during the months of April, 1970 , May , 1970 , June, 1970 , 
July , 1970 , August , 1970 , October , 1970 and December , 1970, 211, 163, 137, 242, 
194 , 197 and 232 ordinary wagons were supplied and all those wagons were manual 
ly loaded . The witness Volunteers that in all those months ordinary wagons 
were supplied only against short rakes and as such as already stated earlier 
the number of such wagons being less , mechanical loading had bcen stopued and 
manual loading was resorted to ; 


To arbitratur . No record has been maintained to indicate the number of 
wagons which were supplied to the colliery with defective brakes. The percen 
tage of supply of such defcclive wagons is very cratic , it varies from 10 % to even 
cent percent, 


Notwithstanding the existence of the third machine which was lying idle the 
management considered it prudent not to get rid of some of the casual wagon 
loaders to avoid labour unrest. As the incidence of breakdown in the machines 
Is also very cratic it is not possible to precisely indicate the requirement of per 
manent wagon loaders even if the management is given free hands to utilise 
both the machines. The third machine which is not in use cannot be used and 
has not been used so far because for putting it into commission some additional 
installations are to be made. It is very difficult to precisely indicate the number 
of days on which the machine remains idle during the month because there had 
been no breakdown at all in some months but there had been months when there 
had been frequent breakdowns and in the first quarter of 1970 one of the 
machines was under breakdown , so far as I remember , for over two months." 

The union has not produced any documentary evidence . The only witness, 
Shri Bancha Mondol (WW . I) . Wagon Loader of Bankola Colllery examined by 
the union has not denied the allegations of the management against the wagon 
loaders regarding forcible manual loading of those wagons which were meant 
for mechanical loading. The witness has also not contradicted the statement of 
WW . I in this regard . In the circumstances , I place reliance on the statement of 
Shri S . K . Singh (MW . I ) . Shri Bancha Mondol (WW . I ) has admitted that, " in 
Bankola Colliery loading work is being done manually as well as mechanically 
and this system of manual as well as mechanical loading has been in existence 
since long . When I joined the colliery in 1962 this was in existence ." Admitted 
ly two machines for loading coal into wagons are in existence at the colliery for 
pretty long time. They have not been introduced presently by the management 
to jeopardise the interest of the workmen in any way. Hence I am fully con 
vinced that the normal capacity of these two machines to load coal into wagons 
cannot be ignored while determining the strength of the permanent wagon load 
erg at the colliery specially when the supply of wagons by the railways in ad 
mittedly very cratic and their loading is time bound by the railways,

This shows that on an average 181 and 623 ordinary and box wagons respectively 
were loaded at Bankola Colliery on an average per month during the year 1970 . 
Admittedly there are 26 working days in a month in a coal mine and as such the 
numbers of ordinary and box wagons loaded at the colliery work out to 7 and 24 
per day respectively . The capacity of two machines is to load twenty box wagons 
per day . This will be evident from Exts . W . 1 ( c ) which shows that on 18th July , 
1970 , 24th July , 1970 , 31st July , 1970 and 18th August, 1970 , 19 - 12, 20 .2 / 3 , 
19 - 112 and 19 - 12 box wagons were loaded mechanically . This has not been chal 
lenged by the union by producing either oral or documentary evidence . So after 
keeping apart 20 box wagons per day for mechanical loading , only seven ordinary 
wagons and four box wagons are left out to be loaded manually . In accordance 
with the directions of the Labour Appellate Tribunal an ordinary wagon can be 
loaded by five persons in eight hours . This work load has also been up - held by 
the Central Wage Board Recommendations for the Coal Mining Industry vide 
paras 52 and 53 , Chapter VIII, Section C of the Coal Wage Board recommenda 
tions. During the course of hearing it was admitted by both parties that on an 
average the capacity of ordinary and box wagons are 22 tons and 55 tons respec 
tively . Accordingly five persons are required to load ordinary wagons and twelve 
persons are required to load box wagons during eight hours. On this basis 35 
loaders are required for loading ordinary wagons and 48 wagon loaders are re 
quired to load four box wagons ( 24 - 20 ) . Thus 83 wagon loaders are required for 
loading the balance wagons left out for manual loading . Provision is to be inade 
for absentceism , leave, sick , etc . In Chapter XV , Section 13 of their recom 
mendations it has been observed by the Central Wage Board for the Coal Mining 
Industry as under : 


" Or the average number of wagons requiring loading per day multiplied by 

the number of loaders required for each wagon . Add to that margia 
of leave and abscntecism which is about 22 per cent." 


Thus for this purpose 22 per cent of the required number of wagon loaders are 
to be provided for which works out to 18 wagon loaders . Provision is also to be 
made for meeting the contingencies of supply of defective wagons and breakdown 
of machincries . The MW . 1 has admitted that the number of defective wagons 
supplied to the colliery is eratic but varies between 10 per cent to cent per cent. 
Regarding the incidence of breakdown of machineries he has stated that, " It is 
very difficult to precisely indicate the number of days on which the machine 
remains idle during a month because there had been no breakdown at all in some 
months but there had been months when there had been frequent breakdowns 
and in the first quarter of 1970 one of the machines was under breakdown , so far 
as I remember, for over two months." There is nothing on record to precisely 
indicate anything in this regard . However , after careful consideration I have 
come to the conclusion that it would be reasonable to provide for an additional 
strength of 24 wagon loaders capable of loading two box wagons, to meet the 
contingencies of supply of defective wagons and break - downs of machines . Thus 
in my opinion 125 (83 plus 18 plus 24 ) should be the strength of permanent wagon 
loaders of Bankola Colliery . 


8 . In the circumstances, I hold that the number of permanent wagon loaders 
at Bankola Colliery of M / s . The Burrækur Coal Co . Ltd ., P . O . Ukhra , Dist . Bur 
dwan shall be 125 . The Arbitration award is made accordingly and submitted to 
the Central Government under Section 10A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 , 


( Sd .) K , SHARAN , 
Regional Labour Commissioner ( ow , Asansol & Arbitrator,

ARBITRATION AWARD 
The employers in relation to the Bararee / Jealgora collleries of Messrs East 
Indian Coal Company Limited , Post office Jeaigora , L hanbad and their workmen . 
represented by the Bihar Coal Miners Union Digwadih , Dhanbad have by the 
written agreement dated the 3rd August , 1970 in pursuance of the provisions of sub 
section ( i) of Section 10A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 agreed to refer the 
industrial dispute existing between them to the arbitration by me and forwarded 
a copy of the said arbitration agreement to the Central Government. The Central 
Government, Ministry of Labour , Employment and Rehabilitation issued the noiifca 
tion .No . 8 / 133 / 10 -LR . II dated 5th December , 1970 and published the said arbitra 
tion agreement at page 5574 of the Gazette of India , Part II, Sec. 3 ( ii) dated 12th 
December , 1970 in persuance of sub - sections ( 3 ) of Section 10 of the Industrial. 
Disputes Act, 1947 . The workmen were represented before me by Shri Pritish 
Chanda , President, Bihar Coal Miners Union and Shri D . I .. Sengupta , Advocate 
and the employers by Shri S , S . Mukherjee, Executive Committee Member, Rani 
ganj Chamber of Commerce . On admission by the employers , Ext. W - 1 for the 
workmen and on admission by the workmen , Exts ,M1 and M 2 for the employers 
were marked . A witness was examined for each party . 

2 . The memorandum of settlement between the marties in Form His Ext. w . 1 
and the Arbitration agreement entered into by them is publißhed at page 5574 of 
the Gazette of India , Part II, Section 3 ( 11) dated 12th December , 1970 . As per the 
settlement and agreement the specific matters in dispute are as follows: 
" ( i) What should be the rate of payment for working on a Sunday, the weekly 

day of rest, in respect of the workmen of the Engineering Department 
of the Bararee and Jealgora collierles of M / s East Indian Coal Co . 

Ltd ., 
( ii) Whether the demand of the workmen for payment for working on Sunday . 

weekly day of rest , at the rate of normal wages for the day plus 14 

time extra is justified ? " 
Admittedly , Sunday is the weekly day of rest for the workmen of the Engineer 
ing Department of Bararee and Jealgora collleries of the employers . The case of 
thē workmen is that the workmen keep the day apart exclusively for domestic 
work , social life and rest and as such if a workman is called upon to work on 
a Sunday there is complete dislocation of his planned life which can be compensated 
Only if he is paid extra wages for the day equivalent to 25 times the daily wages . 
1. e . 11 time extra in addition to his normal wages for the day. The contention of 
the employers is that the workmen of the Engineering Department, whose work is 
not for a period beyond 48 hours in a week Including work on a Sunday is only 
legally entitled to the normal wages alone for the date and not even any overtime 
wages, far less the normal wages plus 11 time extra . At the same timt the 
employers have pointed out that as a matter of fact they are paying at it times 
their normal wages to the workmen for working on a Sunday . According to the 
employers there is no Justification or basis for the claim put forth by the workmen . 
Neither in the pleading : nor in the evidence led by them the parties have clarified 


- 


N 


Sec . 3 (ii) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24, 1971 / VAISAKHA 4 , 1893 2103 
= = - - 

: - SS - - -- 
whether the workmen of the Engineering Department of the Bararee and Jealgora 
Colieries of the employers are underground or surface workinen , I proceed with 
the presumption that all of them are surface workmen . It is also not clarined in 
the pleadings that whether they are morithly rated or daily rated workinen . But 
WW , 1 says that the workmen consist of monthly rated and daily rated . In deter 
r . ining the dispute it in true that I have to bear in mind the inconvenience suficred 
by the workmen when they are made to work on a Sunday , which is their weekly 
rest day and they kcep It apert exclusively icr domestic work , social jite, etc . But. 
al the same time I should not also forget that my inding in this respect will have 
repercussions on other industrial concerns though not directly and my findings 
:llc not binding on them . I should also bear in mind that the effect of my award 
should be aimed at industrial peace and not to sow germ of future litigation or 
disputes in the industrial sphere . The parties have not brought any material on 
record to show the practice prevalent in other collierles in respet of payn : ent of 
wages on Sundays when the workmen are called upon to work on that day . WW . ! 
his simply stated that in T . SCO collieries monit: y rate wurkmen are getting is 
days salary extra for cach Sunday on which they work in ac0111011 to whit they 
get on monthly basis and the daily rated workmen 11 a day s wages for each 
Sunday they work . This oral testimony has no support . 


3 , In respect of payment for work on holidays there was an agreement early in 
1943 between the Indian Mining Association , Indian Mining Federation and the 
Indian Colliery Owners Association on the one hand and labour in the coal mining 
Industry on the other. This is generally known as Joshi Agreement. 
Thiş agreement was adopted and embodied in paragraph 6 of the standing 
orders for the coal mining industry which were certified on 8th April, 1950 . 
A copy of the certified standing order is Ext. M , 1. The overtime rates both under 
the " Joshi Agreeinent ard standing orders were to be in all cases 13 times the 
normai rates of wages . Ir the Indian Mines Act chet! 1! for there was no pru 
vision for overime payment. In the Act which came into operation in 1952 Section 
33 provided for extra wages for overtime and it increased the overtime rates for 
the under ground workers . But this provision has no bearing on the present case , 
inasniuch as the workmen concerned in the present case are all surface wcrkmen . 
Again the question of payment of extra wages for working on paid festivai holidays 
was an issue in the Award of the All Indian Industrial (Colliery Disputes) and 
the Decision of the Labour Appellate Tribunal of India . But neither the Award 
nor the Decision has affected paragraph 6 of the certified standing orders as far 
as wages of surface workmen working on a Sunday , a weekly day of rest is con 
cernci. Paragraph 0 of the certified standing orders for the coal Mining industry , 
Ext, M 1 runs thus : 
" Weekly and daily rated employees shall receive over - time payment at 11 

times the normal daily rate for work on the weekly rest day, recognis 
ed holidays and for overtime work at other days . Those monthly paid 
employees who are entitled to receive payment shall receive overtime 
payment at the rate of 11 times of 1 / 28th of their monthly basic wage 
inclusive of dearness allowance for work on the weekiy rest day 
recognised at the colilery" , 


As I have pointed out earlier the over time rates as per the Standing orders and 
Joshi Agreement" are , in all cases, 14 times the normal rates of wages . It is an 
admitted position that for workmen of daily rate Sundays are off days without 
wages . So, as per the standing orders of a daily rated workman works on a Sunday , 
the weekly rest day, he gets only in times of his formal daily rate of work for 
the day . To be more clear , if his normal daily rate of work is Rs. 5 / - , for having 
worked on a Sunday , the weekly rest day, he gets only Rs. 7. 50 and nothing more . 
On the other hand , if a monthly rated workman works on a Sunday , the weekly 
rest day, he gets his wages for the day at the rate of 1 / 26th of his monthly wages 
plus 11 times of the same. That means, if his monthly wages arę Rs. 26 / - for 
working on a Sunday , the weekly rest day , he gets ( 26 : 26 ) Rs. 1 / - plus Re. 1. 50 , 
total Rs. 2 ,50 . The certified standing orders , Ext. M 1 are still in force and para 
graph 6 thereof is not shown to have been affected by any subsequent award or 
agreement. Section 33 (i ) of the Mines Act , 1952 is substituted by a new sub 
section by virtue of Section 17 of the Act , 1962 of 1959 , whereby if a person works 
in a mine, whether above ground or below ground he is , in respect of the overtime 
work done by him beyond 48 hours in any week , entitled to wages at the rate of 
twice his ordinary rate of wages . But, as I have pointed out above , as per the 
standing orders, Ext . M 1 if a monthly rated workman works on a Sunday , a weekly 
rest day, he gets his wages for the day at the rate of 1 / 26th of his monthly wages 
plus 14 times of the same, 1. e , total of 2 times of his normal daily wage, whereas 
as per the amended section 33 of the Mines Act he gets only twice his normal daily 
wage. As pointed out by WW . 1, out of about 200 workmen of the Engineering

- 


- 


- - 


- - 


- - 


- - 


Department of Bararee and Jealgora collieries 170 or 180 are monthly rated em 
ployees . Application of the present Section 33 of the Mines Act will not be bene 
ficial to the majority of the workmen working in the Engineering Department of 
Bararce and Jcaigora collieries. Of course , daily rated workmen are to get twice 
their normal daily wage under the provision of the Mines Act and only 11 times 
of thçir normal wage under standing orders, Ext. M 1, because for the daily rated 
workmen Sunday, a weekly day of rest is a holiday without wages . Further , Sec 
tion 33 ( 1) of the Mines Act speaks only about the overtime done by a workman 
beyond 48 hours in any week and does not speak specifically for working on a 
Sunday, a weekly rest day . In the absence of any other material I presume that all 
collieries are making payment to their workmen in accordance with paragraph 6 
of the standing orders for working on a Sunday, the weekly rest day, and I do 
not see any reason why a deviation shouid be made in the case of Bararee and 
Jealgora collieries of the employers. This extra payment is in consideration of 
the inconvenience caused to the workmen for working on the weekly rest day and 
I consider this extra payment as sufficient compensation , 

4 . I, therefore, find that in respect of the Engineering Department of the Bararee 
and Jealgora cojlieries of M / s East Indian Coal Conipany the rate of payment to 
workmen for working on a Sunday, the weekly day of rest , should be (1 ) to the 
monthly rated workman his wages for the day at the rate of 1 / 26th of his monthly 
wages, inclusive of his basic wage and dearness allowance pius 11 times of the 
same : and (2 ) to the daily rated workman lt times of his normal dally wage in 
clusive of his busic wage and dearness allowance , 

The settlement, Ext. W , 1 is marked by consent of parties. As per para ( 3 ) of 
the settlement, I hold this award wili be effective t - oin 1st June, 1970 . The award 
is made accordingly and submitted to the Central Government under sub - section ( 4 ) 
of Section 10 - A or The Industrlal Disputes Act, 1947.

का० प्रा० 16 95.-- - यतः इससे उपाबद्ध अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट प्रौद्योगिक विवाद ( जिन्हें इसमें 
इसके पश्चात् उक्त विवाद कहा गया है, थिरु एस० स्वामिषकान्नु , पीठासीन , अधिकारी, प्रौद्योगिक 
अधिकरण , जिसका मुख्यालय भवास है , के समक्ष लम्बित है ; 

और यत : अब थिरु एस० स्वामिक्कान्नु की सेवाएं उपलब्ध नहीं रहीं हैं ; 

अतः, अब , प्रौद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम , 1947 ( 1947 का 14 ) की धारा 7- के और 
धारा 33- ख की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार एतद्वारा एक प्रौद्योगिक अधिकरण गठित करती है जिसके पीठासीन अधिकारी

वाहियों के निपटान के लिए थिरु के० सीथारामा राव , पीठासीन अधिकारी, प्रौद्योगिक 
अधिकरण, मद्रास को इस निदेश के साथ स्थानांतरित करती है कि उक्त अधिकरण और आगे कार्य 
वाहियां उसी प्रक्रम से करेगा जिस पर वे उसे स्थानांतरित की गई हैं और विधि के अनुसार उनका 
नपटान करेगा ।

New Delhi, the 12th April 1971 
S . O . 1696 . - In pursuance of secton 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act , 1947 , 
(14 of 1947 ) , the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of the 
Industrial Tribunal, No. 1 , Dhanbad , in the industrial dispute between the employers 
in relation to the Calcutta Port Commissioners, Calcutta and their workmen , which 
was received by the Central Government on the 2nd April, 1971 , 

Before the Central Government Industrial Tribunal at Dhanbad . 

In the matter of a reference under section 10 (2 ) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947 ( 14 of 1947) . 

REFERENCE No. 55 _ 0F 1968 
PARTIES : 
Employers in relation to the Calcutta Port Commissioners, Calcutta 

AND 
Their workmen . 
PRESENT : 

Shri A . C . Sen, Presiding Officer , 
APPEARANCES : 

For the Employers. --- Shri G . V . Karlekar, Chief Labour Officers .

AWARD 
The above reference is under sub- section ( 2 ) of section 10 of the Industrial 
" Disputes Act and arlses out of order No. 28 / 50 / 68 - LRIII of the Central Govern 
ment dated the 9th July , 1968 . By the said order the following dispute has been 


SEC . 3 (ü ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24 , 1971 /VAISAKHA 4 , 1893 


2107 


- . - - 

- 

- - - - - 
referred to this Tribunal : " Whether the demand of the union that all the members 
of the Crew should be paid overtime and not on individual basis if there is any . 
work during recess hours and if overtime is given on individual basis then those 
of them who are not given overtime should be permitted to leave the vessel is 
justified ? If so , what should be the relief ? 

2 . The relevant facts are briefly stated below . The port of Calcutta is one 
of the major parts governed by the Calcutta Port Act , 1890 . The Commissioners 
for the Port of Calcutta are a body corporate constituted under the said Act . 
The Commisisoners being a local authority the Minimum Wages Act, 1948 became 
applicakle to the Commissioners and the minimum wages were fixed in respect of. 
certain categories of the Commissioner s employees including those employed in the 
crane vessels . 


3 . The workmen concerned are attached to the following crane vessels of the 
Calcutta Port Commissioners: 

( 1 ) C . V . " Atlas" 
( 2 ) C . V . " Vurbahu " 
( 3 ) C . V . " Mahabahu " 
( 4 ) C . V . “ Washington " 

( 51 C . V . " 16 Ton Pon toon Crane" 
4 . The Arst three are self - propelled vessels and the last two are non - propelled 
floating craft . A self- propelled vessel moves about under its own power whereas 
a non -propelled vessel has got to be towed from place to place . 


5 . The workmen attached to the crane vessels " Atlas" , " Virbahu " and " Maha 
bahu " work in twc shifts whereas single shift working 13 In vogue in “ Washington 
and " 10 Ton Pontoon Crane" . The crane vessels work at different points of 
the Docks and the river according to the requirement of the Commissioners for 
loading and un - loading of heavy - lift cargoes or lifting materials , machinery, machi 
nery parts etc . 


6 . Till 30th April, 1954 the crew attached to all crane vessels excepting the 
" 10 Ton Pontoon Crane" which was then known as " 20 Ton Floating Crane" 
Were granted overtime for four hours in each shift which was of 12 hours duration . 
The crew of the " 16 Ton Pontoon Crane" ( then known as 20 Ton Floating Crane ) 
followed workshop hours , i . e ., from 7 - 30 AM to 4 - 30 PM , with a recess of 45 
minutes from 12 - 30 P - M . to 1 - 15 P . M . They were entitled to overtime anly if they 
worked beyond 18 hours a week and overtime was at ordinary rate. 


7 . After the Minimum Wages Act had come into force requiring the Com 
missioner for the Port of Calcutta to pay overtime at twice the ordinary rate for 
work beyond 9 hours a day or 48 hours a week to the members of the crew of the crane 
vessels , they decided to introduce three shifts in those of the vessels where the 
crew worked in double shifts with a view to eliminate overtime. Learning the 
decision of the Commissioners in regard to the matter , the crew of the crene 
vessels represented to the Commissioners in February , 1951 that the old system 
of payment of 4 hours overtime in shifts of 12 hours duration should continue . 


3 . As a result of prolonged negotiation and correspondence between the Com 
missioners and the Union , it was Anally decided that from the 1st May , 1964, the 
crew of the crane vessels would be paid overtime Por 2 hours daily at twice the 
ordinary rate and 2 hours fixed recess , while they would be on 12 hours shifts. 
as the crew of comparable vessels in the Marine Department were entitled to 2 
hours recess in a 12 hours shift , such recess being mutually arranged . 


9 . After the introduction of third shift in the Cargo Docks, the crews of the 
Crane vessels booked for traffic working complained of frequent loss of recess and 
in reply to a representation by the Union for granting them an additional hour s 
ovetime, the Chief Mechanical Engineer by his ietter dated 30th August, 1955 
statd as fc11 . W : : " Regarding additional overtime, if this is to be on a general basis . 
administrative sanction will be necessary but in cases where the crew of any 
particular crew vessel could not be given recess they are being given overtime 
for that period ," 


10. The question of recess hour for the staff of crane vessels was discussed by 
the Chief Mechanical Engineer with the representatives of the staff concerned and 
the General Secretary of the Port Shramik Union in or about March 1958 . The 


2108 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24 , 1971 /VAISAKHA 4 , 1893 [ PART II 


tailowing terus hours for the start were studierte Day the Chie Mechanica 


following recess hours for the staff were suggested by the Chief Mechanical 
Engineer: 

For the Day Shift 
10 - 30 A . M . to 11 - 30 A . M . 

3 -00 P . M , to 4 -00 P . M . 
· For the Night Sbift 

10 -00 P . M . 1o 11 - 30 P . M . 

5 -00 A . M . to 6 -00 A , M . 
The staff of the crane vessels at that time worked in two 12 hour shifts, namely 
6 A , M , to o P . M . and 6 P . M . to 6 A . M . The represantatives of the Union agreed. 
to accept the above timing with the exception of the recess period from 5 A . M . 
to 6 A . M . for the night shift staff . The Chief Mechanical Engineer overruled. 
their objection to the recess period from 5 A . M . to 6 A . M , because he was of 
opinion that the timings for recess were to suit traffic work and that it was 
necessary to introduce the above mentioned timings to suit traffic work . He 
therefore introduced the above mentioned timings for recess with effect froni 
the 11th of March , 1958 (vide Ext. M - 1) . 

11 . During the discussion it was also pointed out by the Chief Mechanica . 
Engineer that the men must continue to work during recess period iť , for any 
reason , a loading or unloading work was in progress and that they must con 
tinue till the job was completed , He further pointed out that as the movement 
of the vessel from place to place was dependent on Dock Master s programme, 
the recess period should be adjusted so that the shipping programme might not 
be hampered . He assured that if one particular recess hour could not be taken 
due to pressure of work , the staff would be entitled to overtime payment as per 
Rules (vide, Ext. M - 1 ) . 

12 . The present dispute originated sometime in 1965 . The version of the 
workmen is as follows. In the year 1965, the union stated recelving complaints 
about denial of overtime to individual members of the crew though the parti 
cular vessels to which such crew members were attached were engaged in work 
during recess hours (vide , para 14 of the Statement by the workmen ) . The 
workmen s Point of view as expressed in their letter dated November 25 , 1965 
to the Chief Mechanical Engineer ( vide Ext. M - 2 ) was follows: " Some men who 
are not paid overtime for work during recess hours are not permitted to leave 
the vessels during such hours. The crews of the vessel work as a team and our 
agreement with the Administration governing payment of overtime to them is 
on collective basis . Our repeated representations or this subject have gone 
unheeded . The financial loss suffered by the men concerned should be made 
good and in future overtime payment should be made in accordance with normal 
rules applicable to the crews of all vessels under the Commissioners." ( vide 
Ext. M - 2 ) . 

13 . The matter was taken up by the Union for discussion with the Chief 
Mechanical Engineer on the 9th of December . 1965 and the ninutes of discusison 
on the subject as recorded by the Chief Mechanical Ergineer read as follows: 
“ Non -payment of ove time to certain members of the crew of all crane vessels 
for working during recess hours : 

" Shri M , L . Chatterjee stated that the Chief Mechanical Engineer was violating 
the agreement with the Administration governing payment of overtime to the 
crew of crane vessels . The men who are not being paid overtime for work during 
recess hours should be permitted to leave the vessels during such hours . 

" C . M . E . stated all agreements were being honoured by him , Enough provi 
sions have been made in the vessels for the crew to spnd their recess hours 
on board the vessels . However , those who were not booked for overtime work 
during recess hours could leave the vessels with the permission of the Engineer 
In Charge keeping in view the safety of the vessels " ( vide Ext. M - 3 ) . 

14 . Thereupon the members of the crew who were not paid overtime though 
their vessels worked during recess hours approached their Engineer -in -Charge 
for permission to leave the vessel during recess hours . The Engineer - in - Charge 
refused permission to the members of the crew to leave the vessel and issued 
the following order in writing : 
" As per the C . M . E s order by phone no body can leave the vessel during 

recess period , otherwise will be punished and charge sheeted ." 
15 . The Union on receipt of the information appraised the Chief Labour OM 
cer of the Calcutta Port Commissioners of the development and addressed a 


SEC . 3( ii ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; APRIL 24 , 1971 /VAISAKHA 4, 1893 


2109 


letter to him , being letter No. 429 / 1202 / E dated 24th December, 1965 conflrming 
the telephonic discussion with him and incorporating the copy of the order men 
tioned in the pevious paragraph . 

18 . Thereafter the Union took up the matter with the higher authorities and 
ultimately agreed to submit the issue for adjudication under section 10 ( 2 ) of the 
Industrial Disputes Act , when no settlement was possible . It is in this way that 
the present dispute has been referred to this Tribunal by the Central Government 
under section 10 ( ? ) of the said Act . 

17 . The main point for consideration is whether all the members of the crow 
should be paid overtime if there is any work during recess hours . The expres 
sion " If there is any work during recess hours really means in the context of 
the facts giving rise to the present dispute " If any crane vessel is requisitioned for 
work during recess hours " . The workmen object to the payment of overtime 
only to some of the members of the crew , leaving out the rest . This method of 
payment of overtime to some and not to all is what they call payment on india 
vidual basis . It is contended on their behalt that the crew of a vessel work as 
a team and not on individual basis and that consequently their hours of work 
are common and no variation can be made in such hours of work in respect at 
individual members of the crew booked for work in a shift . They further con 
tend that in no vessel of other department of the Calcutta Port Commissioners 
discriminotion is made between individual members of the crew la record to 
their hours of work and that all members of the crew working in a sbip or vermel 
are given overtime for equal number of hours or at the same consolidated rate 
of overtime work . 

18 . As stated above the crew of the crane vessels ato allowed 2 hours of 
fixed recess in each shift . It is contended on behalf of the workmen that e . the 
hours of recess for the crew are Axed in crane vessels all members of the crew 
should be on recess during the same period and that all of them whould be paid 
overtime such recess cannot be allowed to all of them at a time. Another 
ground taken by them in support of their claim is that all agreements, under 
standing with the union in regard to the payment of overtime to the members 
of the crew provide for payment of overtime to the crew of particular type 
of vessels on uniform basis and not on individual basis . 

19 . The Commissioners too concede that the members of the crew of the 
crane vessels have to work in a team . But they qualify this concession by saying 
that on sell-propelled crane vessels , different groups of workers employed in 
the vessels carry out work in a team when the vessel 1s being shifted from Dock 
to Dock or from Berth to Berth , or when loading and unloading operations are 
being carried out; and that similarly on non - prepelled vessels also different group 
of workers are required to work during the operations detailed above. 

20 . The position taken up by the Commissioners on this point will be clear if we 
refer to paragraph 7 of the statement filed by them . Part ( 11 ) of Para 7 runs thus : 
When the self-propelled vessels are shifted from Dock to Dock or from Berth to 
Berth , the following workers of the crew generally are required to do the work : 
(1 ) Inland Master , ( li ) Seacunny , ( iii) Dock Lascars , ( v ) Engine Driver , ( vi) Engine 
Room Tindal, (vii) Greaser , ( vili) Coal Tremer , ( ix ) Electrician ( only on occasions 
on Virbahu and Mahabahu ) " . Part (Li) of Paragraph 7 is to the fo !lowing 
effect ; " When the self -propelled vessels are carrying on actual loading and un 
loading operations, the following workers of the crew are required to do work ; 
( i) Crane Driver , ( il ) Winchman , ( ili) Lascars, ( iv ) Electrician (whenever provid 
ed ) , ( b ) Fireman , ( vi ) Greaser, ( vil) Engine Driver ( In case of any break down ) .

any way altered by arrangements cor recreation . The material question is whe 
ther those who are not actually Wiking at any point of tine may be called upon 
por are expected to display physical activity or sustained attention .

30 . Tha forrgoire discussion makes it abundantiv clnar that the demand of the 
union tha ? ! the members of the crew should ho naid overtime and not on indi 
vidual basis iſ there is any work during recess hours is perfectly justified . It is .

- 


- - - 


- 


- - - --- 


therefore , not necessary for me to decide whether the other demand of the union , 
namely , that those of them who are not given overtime should be permitted to 
leave the vessel, is Justified . Moreover the materials on record are not suficient 
to give any decision either way . There is nothing on record to show that the 
members of the crew are entitled to leave the vessel even when there is no ques 
tion of working during recess . According to the Harbour Master , every Irland 
vessel should be fully manned and ready to act in case of emergency and all the 
crew are to act in case of emergency . It is an offence it they are not on their 
vessels . This seems to indicate that the crew cannot leave the vessel as of right 
even when they are not required to work during recess hours ; if that be so , It is 
all the more reason why they should not be allowed to leave the vessel when it 
iş requisitioned for loading or unloading during fixed recess hours. However , I 
do not come to any definite finding on this point, nor is it necessary for me to do 
So . I may however point out that in this case those who were not paid overtime 
sought for permission to leave the vessel, but that permission was refused . This 
strengthens the claim of the workers that all the members should be paid overtline 
if there is any work during recess hours . 

31. My award is that the demand of the union that all the members of the 
crew should be paid overtime and not on individual bagis if there is any work 
during recess hourg is justified and that overtime is to be paid on this basis from 
the date of the award ,

rospect to the said draft before the date so specified will be taken into considera 
tion by the Central Government.

tion of Employment) Amendment Scheme, 1971 ,

and other allowances and thereafter the Chairman in exceptional cases , 
grant higher subsistence allowance not exceeding three - fourths of such 

dally wage including dearness and other allowances : 
Provided that where such enquiry is prolonged beyond a period of ninety days 

for reasons directly attributable to the worker , the subsistence allow 
ance shall, for the period exceeding ninety days. be reduced to one : 

fourth of the daily wage including dearness and other allowances . " ;

S . O . 1698 . -- The following draft of a scheme further to amend the Visakhapat 
nam Unregistered Dock Workers (Regulation of Employment) Schenie , 1968 , which 
the Central Government proposes to make in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub - section ( 1 ) of section 4 of the Dock Workers ( Regulation of Employment) 
Act , 1948 ( 9 of 1948 ) , is published as required by the said sub - section for the tri 
formation of all persons likely to be affected thereby ; and notice is hereby given 
that the said draft will be taken into consideration on or after the 2011 May, 1971. 


Any objections or suggestions which may be received from any person with 
respect to the said draft before the date so specified will be taken into considera 
tion by the Central Government, 


DRAFT SCHEME 
1 . This Scheme may be called the Visakhapatnam Unregistered Dock Workers 
( Regulation of Employment) Amendment Scheme, 1971. 


2 . In the Visakhapatnam Unregistered Dock Workers ( Regulation of Employ 
ment) Scheme, 1968 ( i) in clause 32 - A , for item ( a ) of sub - clause (5 ) tne follow 
ing item shall be substituted by the following ; namely : 
" ( a ) Where a worker has been suspended pending enquiry , he shall be paid 

for the first ninety days from the date of suspension , a subsistence 
allowance equivalent to one-half of the daily wage inc ding deerness 
and other allowances and thereafter the Chairman in exceptional 
cases, grant higher subsistence allowance not exceeding three- fourths 

of such daily wage including dearness and other allowances : 
Provided that where such enquiry is prolonged beyond a period of ninety 

days for reasons directly attributable to the worker, the subsistence 
allowance shall, for the period exceeding ninety days , be reduced to 
one - fourth of the daily wage including dearness and other allowances." 


(ii) In clauscs 14 -AA for item (111) ( a ) of sub - clause (2 ) the following item 

shall be substituted namely : 
( ili ) ( a ) Where a worker has been suspended pending enquily , he shall be 

paid for the first ninety days from the date of suspension , a subsis 
lence allowance equivalent to one -half of the daily wage including 
dearness and other allowances and thereafter the Chilrnan in excep . 
tional cases, grant higher subsistence allowance not exceeding three. 
tourths of such daily wages including dearness and over allowances 


Provided that where such enquiry is prolonged beyond a period of ninety 

days for reasons directly attributable to the worker , the subsistence 
allowance shall, for the period excecding ninety days, be reduced to 
one- fourth of the daily wage including dearness and other allowan .

का० प्रा० 1700-~ - मुम्बई अरजिस्ट्रीकृत रक कर्मकार नियोजन का विनियन ) स्कीम , 1957 
में और प्राग संशोधन करने के लिए केन्द्रीय सरकार डपक कर्मकार ( नियोजन का विनियमन ) 
अधिनियम, 1948 ( 1948 का 9 ) की धारा 4 के उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग 
करते हुए जिस स्कीम को वनान की प्रस्धापना करती है, उसका निम्नलिखित प्रारूप , उक्त 
उपधारा द्वारा ययाअपेक्षित, उन सभी व्यक्तियों की जानकारी के लिए प्रकाशित किया जाता है 
जिनका ततद्वारा प्रभावित होना संभाव्य है, और एतद्वारा सूचित किया जाता है कि उक्त प्रारूप 
तर 26 - 5 - 71 को या उसके पश्चात् विचार किया जाएगा । 

। उक्त प्रारूप के बारे में जो आक्षेप या सुझाव किसी व्यक्ति से ऐसी विनिर्दिष्ट तारीख से 
पुत्र प्राप्त होंग उन पर केन्द्रीय सरकार द्वारा विचार किया जाएगा ।

संशोधन स्कीम , 1971 होगा ।

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 13th April 1971 
S . O . 1701 . -- Whereas the Central Government is of opinton that an indus 
trial dispute exists between the employers in relation to the Messrs. Peter and 
Smith ( India ) Private Limited , Calcutta and their workmen in respect of the 
matters specified in the Schedule here to annexed ; 

And whereas the Central Government considers it desirable to refer the said 
dispute for adjudication ; 

Now , therefore, in cxercise of the powers conferred by clausc ( ) of sub 
section ( 1 ) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act 1947 ( 14 of 1947) , the 
Central Government hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal, Calcutta , constituted under section 7A of the 
Said Act . 

SCHEDULE 
I . " Whether the action of the manugement in terminating the services of Shri 
S . Thol, Jetty Sircar , with effect from the 1st December , 1970 was wrongful and 


illegal. 


If so , to what rellef if any, is the workman entitled ? "

1966 by the management of Messrs . Peters and Smith ( India ) Private Limited , 
Calcutta , and if so , whether the action of the management in terminating the ser 
vices of Shri Shaw with effect from 1st December , 1970 was wronglu ) and Illegal . 
If so , to what relief , if any, is the workman antiled ? "

(Department of Labour and Employment) 

New Delhi, the 13th April 1971 
S . O . 1702. - Whereas M / s. Toy Menka Kalsha Limited , No . 198, Jamshedji 
Tata Road, Bombay - 1 ( hereinafter referred to as the sald establishment ) have 
applied for cxemption under clause ( a ) of sub -section ( 1 ) of section 17 of the 
Employees Provident Funds Act , 1952 ( 19 of 1952 ) ; 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Government, the rules of the 
provident fund of the said establishment with respect to the rates of contribution 
are no less favourable to the employees therein than those specified in section 6 
of the said Act , and the employees are also in enjoyment of other provident fund 
benefits which on the whole are not less favourable to the employees than the 
benefits provided under the said Act or under the Employees Provident Funds 
Scheme, 1952 (hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme) in relation to the 
employees in any other establishment of a similar character ; 
___ Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause ( a ) of sub-section 
( 1 ) of Section 17 of the said Act . and subject to the conditions specified in the 
Schedule annexed hereto , the Central Government hereby exempts the said establ. 
ishment from the operation of all the provisions of the said Schere, and in purgu 
ance of sub-section ( 3 ) of the said Section 17 , the Central Government hereby 
directs that, 
( a ) the employer in relation to the said establishment shall pay within fifteen 

days of the close of the month to the Employees Provident Fund ,

THE SCHEDULE 
1 . The employer shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner as the Central Goverment may, from time to time, prescribe. 

2 . The employer shall furnish to each employee an Annual Statement of Account 
or Pass Book . 

3 . All expenses involved in the administration of the Fund including the main 
tenance of accounts , submission of accounts and returns, transfer of accumula 
tions, payment of inspection charges etc ., shall be borne by the employer . 

4 . The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the establishment & copy 
of the Rules of the Fund as approved by the appropriate Government and , as and 
when amended alongwith a translation of the salient points thereof in the language 
of the majority of the employees . 

5 . Wbere an employee who is already member of the Employees Provident 
Fund ( Statutory Fund ) or the Provident Fund of another exempted establishment 
is employed in his establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as 
a member of the Fund of the establishment, and accept the past accumulations 
in respect of such employees and credit to his account. 

6 . The employer shall enhance the rate of provident fund contribution appro 
priately if the rate of provident fund contributions for the class of establishmenta 
in which his establishment falls is enhanced under the Employees Provident Funds 
Act, 1952 so that the beneftg under the provident fund Scheme of the establish 
ment shall not become less favourable than the benefit provided under the 
Employee s Provident Funds Act , 1952. 

7. The establishment shall submit an audited balance sheet of its provident 
fund every year to the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner within 3 months 
of the close of the year . 

8 . No amendment of the Rules of the provident fund shall be made without the 
previous approval of the Central Provident Fund Commissioner . Where any 
amendment is likely to affect adversely the interests of the employee , the Central 
Provident Fund Commissioner shall, before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their point of view ,

__ 1. नियोजक क्षेत्रीय भविष्य निधि प्रायुक्त को वे विवरणियां भेजेगा जिन्हें केन्द्रीय सरकार

बहुसंख्या के मापा उसकी गख्य मुख्य बातों का प्रन बाद भी प्रदर्शित करेगा ।

नियोजक क निधि के : यस्य के रूप में उसका नाम तुरन्त ही दर्ज करेगा और ए से कर्मचारी 
की बाबत नसके पिछले संचयं को स्वीकार कर लसके खाते में जमा करेगा ।

कर्मचारी भविष्य निधि अधिनियम , 1952 के अधीन है ।

निधि आयक्त को वषन्ति के तीन मास के भीतर भेजेगा ।

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; APRIL 24 , 1971 /VAISAKHA 4, 1893 [Part II — 


(Department of Labour and Employment) 

New Delhi, the 15th April 1971 
S . O . 1706 . - In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 ( 14 
of 1947) the Central Government hereby publishes the following Award of the 
Central Government Labour Court, Delhi in respect of a complaint under section 
33A of the said Act filed by Shrimati Sarla Jain , Pharmacist, Employees State 
Insurance Corporation Dispensary , Mehraull, New Delhi, which was received by 
the Central Government on 7th April, 1971 . 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT LABOUR COURT, DELHI 
PRESENT : 
Shri R . K . Baweja , Central Government Labour Court, Delhi, 

31st March , 1971 / 10th Chait, 1892 (S ) 
Complaint U / S 33A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 


COMPLAINT I. D . NO. 1 OF 1971 
. . Smt. Sarla Jain W / o Shri R . K . Jain , Pharmacist, E . S .I. Corporation Dis 

pensary , Mehrauli , New Delhi C / o Hospital Employees Union , H .O . 
Aggarwal Bhavan , G . T . Road , Tees Hazari, Delhi. - Complaint. 


Versus 
Employees State Corporation , Kotla Road , New Delhi. Respondent , 
Shri C . P , Aggarwal for the complainant. 
Shri Jaswant Rai - for the respondent. 
In the Matter of LCA No. 58 of 1970 

AWARD 
The complainant Smt. Sarla Jain filed an application under section 38C ( 2 ) of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (LCA No . 58 of 1970 ) , in this Court and claimed 
house rent allowance for the period from 1st March 1970 to 30th April 1970 , 
which she alleged had been discontinued by her employer, the employees State 
Corporation , Kotla Road , New Delhi, (hereinafter to be referred as respondent) . 
That application is still pending in this Court. It is alleged in the complaint filed 
by her that the respondent issued orders on the 11th of February , 1971 wherein 
& direction was issued for the recovery of the whole amount paid to the com 
plainant by way of house rent allowance prior to 1st March , 1970 . The com 
plant further continued that during the pendency of the application under sec 
tion 33C ( 2 ), the respondent passed the order of recovery of rent in violation of 
the provisions of section 33 of the Act. It was , therefore , prayed that the com 
plaint be dealt with according to law and the respondent be restrained from tak 
ing any action against her for recovering the said amount till the final disposal 
of her application filed under section 33C ( 2 ) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 , 


2 . The respondent in its reply inter alia raised the objection that as there was 
no Industrial dispute between the parties pending before this court , it could not 
be said that the provisions of section 33 of the Act had been contravened and 
that the complaint under section 33A of the Act was not maintainable . A re 
Joinder was also filed on behalf of the complainant in which it was reiterated 
that an industrial dispute between the parties existed in view of the pendency 
of the application under section 33C ( 2 ) of the Act. 


3 . It was admitted by Shri Aggarwal who represented the complainant that 
no reference under section 10 of the Act had been made by the Government for 
adjudication of an industrial dispute existing between the parties . The relevant 
portion of Section 33 of the Act reads as follows: 
" During the tendency of any conciliation proceeding before a conciliation 

officer or a Board or of any proceeding before a Labour Court or 
Tribunal or National Tribunal in respect of an industrial dispute , no 

employer shall 
( a ) in regard to any matter connected with the dispute , alter , to the pre 

Judice of the workmen concerned in such dispute , the conditions of 
service applicable to them immediately before the commencement 
of such proceeding ; or 


Sec . 3 (1 ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24 , 1971 / VAISAKUA 1. 1893 2125 
- .- -.- - : - -- - - - - - -- .. . - - -- . - . _ ." . 

- - ---- --- --- -- - - - - - - -- 
( b ) for any misconduct connected with the dispute , discharge or punish , 

whether by dismissal or otherwise , any workmen concerned in such 

dispute . 
Save with the express permission in writing of the authority before 

which the proceeding is pending. 
Section 33A lays down that where an employer contravenes the provisions of 
section 33 during the pendency of proceedings before a Labour Court, Tribunal or 
National Tribunal, any employee aggrieved by such contravention , may make a 
complaint in writing, in the prescribed manner to such Labour Court , Tribunal 
or National Tribunal and on receipt of such complaint that Labour Court, Tribunal 
or National Tribunal shall adjudicate upon the complaint as if it were a dispute 
referred to or pending before it , in accordance with the provisions of this Act and 
shall submit its award to the appropriate Government and the provisions of this 
Act shall apply accordingly . So, it is crystal clear from a perusal of the abuve 
provisions that it i" necessary for the complainant to prove that there was a jen 
dency of an industrial dispute and that during that pendency the employer has 
altered the conditions of service to his prejudice and has discharged , punished or 
otherwise terminated his services for any misconduct without the prior permission 
of the Tribunal or Board or the Labour Court concerned . If there is no industrial 
dispute pending then the provisions of sections 33 and 33A will not be applicable . 
An application under section 33C ( 2 ) enables an employee to file his claim in the 
Labour Court specifically empowered for that purpose for the recovery of money 
due from an employer . Such an application does not embrance within its sweep 
any industrial dispute . The expression " industrial disputc " has been defined in 
section 2 ( k ) of the Act and when it arises between an employer and his em 
ployee , it is referred for adjudication by the Government as provided in section 
10 of the Act . It cannot be referred direct by an employee to any Court or 
Tribunal. In my view , therefore, the complainit under section 33A is not main 
tainable as no industrial dispute between the parties is pending before any Labour 
Court or Tribunal. Shri C . P . Aggarwal who represented thc complainant, how 
ever, relied on a dispute between M . Ramasubhu and Rani Motor Works, Tiru 
nelveli and another ( 1964 , I. LLJ . 249 ) . I fail to understand as to how this ruling 
helps the complainant. The question before the Madras High Court in that case 
was whether an employee in proceedings under section 33C ( 2 ) of the Act could 
raise an objection regarding the engagement of a llwyer by the eniploger within 
the meaning of section 36 ( 4 ) of the Act . It was held that a proceeding under 
section 33C ( 2 ) of the Act involved a dispute within the meaning of sub - section 
( 4 ) of Section 36 of the Act. It is to be noticed that the word used in that suh 
section is " dispute " and not an " industrial dispute " . So , when an employee Alcs 
an application against his employer that he is entitled to certain benefits , which 
can be computed in terms of money , certainly there is a dispute but it is not an 
industrial dispute of which we find a reference in section 33 . Every dispute is 
not an industrial dispute . I am , thereforc, clear in my mind that the complaint 
under section 33A of the Act is not maintainable . The objection of the respondent 
is upheld and the complaint is dismissed . 
( Four pages ) 
31st March , 1971 . 

( Sd .) R . K , BAWEJA , 
Central Government Labour Court, Delhi.

का० प्रा० 1706. - न्यतः इससे उपाबद्ध अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट प्रौद्योगिक विषाद श्री 
बी० एम० जयमहादेव प्रसाद, पीठासीन अधिकारी, प्रौधोगिक अधिकरण , बंगलौर के समक्ष 
लम्बित है ; 

और यतः श्री बी० एम० जयमहादेव प्रसाद की सेवाएं अब उपलब्ध नहीं रही हैं ; 

प्रतः, अब , प्रौद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम , 1947 ( 1947 का 14 ) की धारा 7 - क और 
33 - ख की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद 
मारा एक प्रौद्योगिक प्रधिकरण गठित करती है जिसके पीठासीन अधिकारी श्री नारायण राय 
कदर और म ख्यालय बंगलौर होंगे तथा उक्त विवादों के प्रबन्ध में कार्यवाहियों को श्री बी . 
एम . जयमहादेव प्रसाद से प्रत्याहस करती है और उन्हें उक्त प्रौद्योगिक अधिकरण , बंगलोर को 
उक्त कार्यवाहियों के निपटान के लिए इस निदेश के साथ अन्तरित करती है कि उक्त प्रधिकरण 
कार्यवाहियों पर उस प्रक्रम से कार्यवाही करेगा जिस पर वे उसे अन्तरित की गई हैं और विधि 
के अनुसार उनका निपटान करेगा ।

का० प्रा० 1797. -- प्रतः इससे उमाबद्ध अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट प्रौद्योगिक विवाद केन्द्रीय 
सरकार प्रोद्योगिक प्रधिकरण 2, मुम्बई के समक्ष लम्बित है ; 

और यतः विवाद से सम्बन्धित कर्मकार ने उस मामले को मुम्बई से अहमदाबाद स्थानान्तरित 
करने के लिए केन्द्रीय सरकार से प्रार्थना की है ; 

और यत : केन्द्रीय सरकार प्रार्थना को उचित मानती है ; 

अतः, अब , औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम , 1947 ( 19 47 का 14 ) की धारा 7- क और 
धारा 33- ख की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद्वारा एक 
प्रौद्योगिक अधिकरण गठित करती है जिसके पीठासीन अधिकारी श्री आई० जी० ठाकुर होंगे , जिसका 
म ख्यालय अहमदाबाद होगा , उक्त विवाद से संबंधित कार्यवाहियों को , जो केन्द्रीय सरकार प्रौद्योगिक 
अधिकरण- 2, मुम्बई के समक्ष लम्बित है, प्रत्याहृत करती ह और उन्हें उस गठित किए गए प्रौद्योगिक 
अधिकरण को , जिसके पीठासीन अधिकारी श्री पाई ० जी० ठाकुर हैं , स्थानान्तरित करती है और 
निदेश देती है कि उक्त अधिकरण उक्त कार्याहियों पर उस प्रक्रम से अगे कार्यवाही करेगा जिम 
पर वे उसे स्थानान्तरित की गई है और विधि के अनुसार उनका निपटारा करेगा ।

under clause b ) of sub - section ( 2) of section 33 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) 
are pending before Shri S . C . Bar4a , presiding Officer, Industrial Tribunal, Dibrugarh ; 

And whereas the services of Shri S . C . Barua have ceased to be available ; 

And whereas the CentralGovernment has constituted an Industrial Tribunal with Shri G . N . 
Borah as the Presiding Officer with headquarters at Dibrugarh ; 

. Now , therefore, in epircise of th : powers conferred by gub - section (1 ) of section 33B 
of the ghid Act ,theConttalGovernment hereby withdraws the proceedings in relation tothe said 
applications from S . C . Barua and transfers the same to Shri G . N . Borah , Presiding Officer , 
Ind 19trial Trib1121 ,Dibrigach for the disposal of the said proceedings with the direction that the 
said Trib :inal shall proceed with each ofthe said applications from the stage at which it la trans 
ferred and dispose of the same according to law .

KARNAIL SINGH , Under Secy. 

नई दिल्ली , 14 अप्रैल , 1971 
एस० वी० 1714. ---यतः प्रौधोगिक विवाद अधिनियम , 1947 ( 1947 का 14 ) की धारा 
33 की उप-धारा ( 2 ) के खण्ड ( ख ) के अधीन दिए गए इससे उपाबद्ध अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट प्रावेदन 
श्री एस० सी० बरुया, पीठासीन अधिकारी , प्रौद्योगिक प्रधिकरण , डिबगड़ के समक्ष लम्बित है ; 

और यतः श्री एस० सी० बरुपा की सेवाएं उपलभ्य नहीं रही हैं ; 

और याः केन्द्रीय सरकार ने एक प्रौद्योगिक प्रधिकरण गठित किया है जिस के पीठासीन 
अधिकारी श्री जी० एन० बोराह होंगे और जिनका मख्यालय डिब्रुगढ़ में होगा । 

अतः, अब , उपर्युक्त अधिनियम की धारा 33 ख की उप -धारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का 
प्रयोग करते हुए, केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद्वारा श्री एस०सी०बरमा से उक्त प्रावेदनों से सम्बद्ध कार्यवाहियों 
को वापिस लेती है और उन्हें उक्त कार्यवाहियों के निपटान के लिए श्री जी० एन० बोराह , पीठासीन 


SEc . 3 (ii ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : APRIL 24, 1971/ VAISAKHA 4, 1893 


2135 


अधिकारी, प्रौद्योगिक अधिकरण , डिब्रुगढ़ को इस निदेश के साथ स्थानान्तरित करती है कि उक्त अधि - , 
करण उक्त प्रावेदनों में से प्रत्येक पर और आगे कार्यवाही उस प्रक्रम से करेगा, जिस पर वह उस स्था 
नान्तरित किया जाए और विधि के अनुसार उसका निपटान करेगा ।

(Department of Labour and Employınent) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 17th April 1971 
S . O . 1715. - Whereas an industrial dispute exists between the employers in rela 
tion to the management of Bhilai Steel Plan , Bhilai, and their workmen represent 
ed by Mines Workers Union , Rajhara Mines, P . O . Dalli Rajhara , Distt. Durg 
( M . P . ) ; 

And whereas the said employers and their workmen have by a written agree 
ment , in pursuance of the provisions of sub -Section ( i ) of section 10A of the Indus 
trial Disputes Act , 1947 ( 14 of 1947) referred the said dispute to arbitration by the 
person specifient therein , and a copy of the sait arbitration agreement has been 
forwarded to the Central Government; 

Now , therefore in nursuaren of the provisions of sub -section ( 3 ) of section 10A of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947 ) , the Central Government hereby 
publishes the said arbitration agreement , which was recelved by it on the 29th 
March , 1971. 

Agreement 
( U / S 10A OF THE I. D . ACT . 1947 ) 

BETWEEN

(Mines ) , Bhilai Steel Plant, P . O . Bhilal -1 ( M . P . ). 
2. Shri K . G . Marar , Labour Welfare Officer ( Mines ), Bhilai Steel Plant,

Distt. Durg , (M . P .). 


Il is hereby agreed between the parties to refer the following industrial dispute 
to the arbitration of Shri N . D . Bodade, Regional Labour Commissloner ( Central) , 
Jabalpur. 

1. Specific matter in dispute . - -Whether or not Shri B . R . Pillai, Attendant, is 
entitled to the post of Head Time Keeper and iſ so froin what date , bearing in mind 
the repercussions on others in the Time Keeping Section . 

2 . Detalls of the parties to the dispute including the name and address of the 
establishment or undertaking involved . 

General Manager, Bhilal Steel Plant, Hidustan Steel Limited , P. O . Bhilai -1 , 
( M .P . ) in relatiori to Rajhara Mechanised Mines and their workman represented 
by Mines Workers Union, Rajhara Branch, P . O . Dalli Ri.jhara. Distt . Durg . ( M . P . ) . 

3 . Name of the Union , if any , representing the wuskinian In question , 
A . Mines workers Union . Rajhara Branch, P. O . Dalli- Rajharn , Distt. Durg ( M. P. ) . 
5 . Total number of workmen employed in the undertaking : 

3091 ( Three thousand and ninety one). 
6 . Estimated number of workmen affected or likely to be affected by the dispute ; 

1 ( One ) . 


The arbitrator shall make his award within a period of 4 ( four ) months or 
within such further time as is extended by mutual agreoment between us in writing . 
In case the itWird is not made within the period aforementioned , the reference to 
arbitration shall stand automatically cancelled and we shall be free to negotiate 
for fresh arbitration.

का० प्रा० 1715. - - यत : भिलाई स्टील प्लांट भिलाई के प्रबन्धतंत्र से सम्बद्ध नियोजकों और 
उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, जिनका प्रतिनिधित्व खनिक श्रमिक यूनियन , राजहरा खानें , डाकघर डल्ली 
राजहरा,जिला दर्ग ( मध्य प्रदेश ) करती है, एक प्रौद्योगिक विवाद विद्यमान है ; 


और यत : उक्त नियोजकों और कर्मकारों ने प्रौद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम , 1947 ( 1947 
का 14 ) की धारा 10- क की उपधारा ( 1 ) के उपबन्धों के अनुसरण में एक लिखित करार द्वारा 
उका विवाद को उसमें वणित व्यक्ति के माध्यस्थम के लिए निर्देशित करने का करार कर लिया है 
और उक्त माध्यस्थ्म करार की एक प्रति केन्द्रीय सरकार को भेजी गई है ;

की धारा 17 के खंड ( 4 खख ) तथा खंड ( क ) तथा धारा 46 ग की उप - धारा ( 2 ) 
के खंड ( क ) के द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए , केन्द्रीय सरकार एतत्वारा भारतीय 
प्रौद्योगिक पुनर्निमाण निगम लिमिटेड को , जो समवाय अधिनियम , 1956 ( 1956 का 
पहला ) के अन्तर्गत स्थापित तथा जीकृत कम्पनी है , उपयुक्त खडा के प्रयोजनों के लिये एक 
वित्तीय संस्था के रूप में अधिसूचित करती है ।

PART II 


s . o . 1720 . - In exercise of the powers conterred by section 53 of the Banking 
Regulation Act, 1949 ( 10 of 1949 ) , the Central Government, on the recommenda 
tion of the Reserve Bank of India , hereby declares : 
( a ) that so much of the provisions of sub - clause ( i) and sub - clause ( ii ) of 

clause ( c ) of sub -section ( 1 ) of section 10 of the Banking Regulation 
Act , 1949 , af relate to person managing any of tlie banks specified in 
the schedule to this notification shall not apply to a director on the 
Board of the Industrial Reconstruction Corporation of India Limited , 
a company formed and registered under the Companies Act, 1956 

(1 of 1956) ; and 
( b ) that the provisions of sub - section ( 3 ) of section 19 of the said Act shall 

not apply to any of the banks specified in the said Senedule in so 
far as the said provisions relate to the holding of shares in the said 
Industrial Reconstruction Corporation of India Limited . 

THE SCHEDULE 
( 1) The State Bank of India ; 
(II ) any subsidiary bank as defined in clause ( k ) of section 2 of the State 

Bank of India ( Subsidiary Banks ) Act , 1959 ( 38 of 1859 ) , 
( 111 ) any corresponding new bank constituted under section 3 of the Bank 

ing Companies ( Acquisition and Transfer of Undertaking ) Act , 1970 

( 5 of 1970 ) ; 
( lv ) any banking company as defined in clause ( c ) of section 5 of the 
Banking Regulation Act, 1949 ( 10 of 1949 ). 

[ No. F . 1 ( 107 ) - Corp / 70 / IV . ] 

N. SETURAMAN. 
Director to the Government of India . 


सा० प्रा० 1720 .-.- सांविधिक आदेश : बैंकिंग विनियमन अधिनियम , 1949( 1949 का 
दसवां ) की धारा 5 3 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार . भारतीय रिजर्व 
बैंक की सिफारिश पर एतद् द्वारा घोषित करती है - - 

( क ) कि बैंकिंग विनियमन अधिनियम , 1949 की धारा 10 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के 

खड ( ग ) के उपखंड (1 ) और उपखंड ( । ) के वे उपबन्ध , जिनका इस 
अधिसूचना की अनुसूची में उल्लिखित बैंकों में से किसी भी बैंक का प्रबन्ध 
करने वाले व्यक्तियों से सम्बन्ध है , भारतीय प्रौद्योगिक पुननिमाण निगम 
लिमिटेड , जो कम्पनी अधिनियम , 1956 ( 1956 का पहला ) के अन्तर्गत 
स्थापित तथा पंजीकृत कम्पनी है , के बोडों के निदेशक पर लाग नहीं होंगे ; और 


( ख ) कि उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 19 की उपधारा ( 3 ) के उपबंध , जहां तक 

उनका सम्बन्ध भारतीय प्रौद्योगिक पुननिमाण निगम लिमिटेड के शेयर प्राप्त 
करने से हैं , उक्त अनुसूची में उल्लिखित बैंकों में से किसी भी बैंक पर लागू 
नहीं होंगे ।

( Certificate Proceedings) Rules , 1962, the Central Government hereby appoints 
the Commissioner of Income- tax , Orissa , Bhubaneswar to be the Tax Recovery 
Commissioner , Orissa.

एस० प्रो . 1722. - - प्रायकर अधिनियम , 1961 ( 1961 का 43 ) की धारा 2 के 
खण्ड ( 44 ) के उप- खण्ड (।।। ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद्वारा 

1. श्री बी० एन० महान्ति और 

2 . श्री के० अप्पा राव 
को , जो केन्द्रीय सरकार के राजपत्रित अधिकारी , हैं उक्त अधिनियम के अधीन कर वसूली अधिकारियों 
की शक्तियों का प्रयोग करने के लिए प्राधिकृत करती है ।

जिन करदाताओं पर पहली अप्रैल , 1969 से 31 मार्च, 1970 तक की अवधि में करन 
भरने के कारण , 5000 रुपये या उससे अधिक अर्थदण्ड (जर्माने ) हा है और निर्धारित अवधि में 
उन्होंने उसके विरुद्ध कोई अपील नहीं की या अपील की परन्तु उनके मामले में वित्त वर्ष 1969-- 
70 के लिए इस जुर्माने को निश्चित मान लिया गया है ।

S . O . 1735.: - In pursuance of sub-section( I ) of Section 287 of the Income -tex Act , 1961 ( 43 of 
1961), the Central Government , being of opinion that it is expedient in the public interest soto 
do hereby publishes the names of the assessees . 
( a ) being individuals or Hindu undivided families who have been asesssed on an income . 

of more than a lakh of rupees ; 
( b ) being firms, associations of persons and companies whohave been asscoged on an income

प्राकी गई है ।

करना समयोचित होगा : 

( अ ) 1969- 70 वित्त वर्ष में ऐसे व्यक्ति या हिन्दु संयूक्त परियार जिनकी प्राय एक 

लाख रुपये से अधिक की गई है । 
( ब ) ऐसी फर्म, व्यक्तियों के संगठन , और कम्पनियों जिनकी आय दस लाख रुपये से 

अधिक आंकी गई है ।

रुपये से अधिक प्रांकी गई है ।
